# Agent 3 — Regulation & Deadlines — Findings 2026-08-27

(Method: shared research harness — one agent per regulation workstream, adversarial per-claim verification, completeness gate, DETERMINISTIC table render. 11 regulation workstream(s) targeted, 11 returned signals, 141 verified signals. Confidence capped at Medium per single-agent playbook. Table rows ordered by Printec value (size x win), biddable first.)

## What changed since last run

The single biggest new item is ECB letter SSM-2026-0301, signed by Claudia Buch on 7 July 2026, on AI-enabled cybersecurity threats — confirmed verbatim at primary text and directly relevant to every significant institution in AT, BG, CY, GR, HR, SI and SK; its closing paragraph also pre-announces a second ECB letter on post-quantum cryptography, which is a forward signal and not yet a deadline. Second, the AML build-out is now dated and real: AMLA's Art.28(1) AMLR customer-due-diligence RTS were due to the Commission by 10 July 2026, a Cellar SPARQL query independently confirmed the Level-2 pipeline, four AMLA consultations are open with hard dates, and the 2027 direct-supervision selection pipeline is running through national supervisors. Third, digital euro front-end work has hardened: the ECB signed standardisation agreements with CPACE/nexo/Berlin Group in April 2026, a fourth MoU with the EPC is being formalised, and rulebook v0.91 Annex D1 #4 §7 now specifies three ATM functions (manual funding, defunding, and related handling) — a concrete terminal-side spec. Fourth, national cash and ATM regimes moved: Hungary's ATM decommissioning and relocation freeze has been in force since 14 May 2026, Slovenia's draft ZDUPS closed consultation on 17 August 2026 and entered negotiation with Banka Slovenije and ZBS on 24 August, and Bulgaria's dual-display period ended 8 August 2026 with KZP/NRA control continuing under a changed legal basis. On corrections: the previous run's "baseline date correction" for RO/CZ/HU instant payments under Reg 260/2012 Art.5a(8) was itself wrong and is reversed; the BNB TARGET-BNB participant count was corrected; Bulgaria's licensed CASP count is four, not two; the Slovenian draft law was not renamed; and the DORA Register of Information date of 31/12/2026 is a reference date, not a submission deadline. Items that could not be confirmed and should not be quoted client-facing include the Romanian "OUG 5/2026" number, Montenegro's AML amendment date, Bulgaria's FATF determination, the Bulgarian EUDI wallet draft law, and the Greek/Cypriot/Romanian MiCA press claims.

## Top opportunities (ranked)

1. ECB SSM significant institutions (AT, BG, CY, GR, HR, SI, SK) — AI-threat security hardening and controls uplift against SSM-2026-0301 (XL, Medium).
2. EU-footprint banks (AT, BG, HR, CY, CZ, EL, HU, RO, SK, SI) — AMLR Art.28(1) CDD/onboarding and identity-verification remediation ahead of the RTS (XL, Medium).
3. National AML supervisors and their banks (BNB, HNB, CBC, CNB-CZ, NBS-SK, MNB, BNR, BSI, BoG, FMA-AT) — AMLA reporting-package and 2027 direct-supervision readiness tooling (XL, Medium).
4. EU-footprint banks — AMLA/Commission Level-2 change-tracking and compliance-implementation programme (XL, Medium).
5. NCA-supervised banks in HR, GR, CY, BG, RO, AT, SI, SK, CZ, HU — DORA Art.26 threat-led penetration testing (TLPT) delivery (L, Medium).
6. ECB SSM significant institutions (AT, BG, CY, GR, HR, SI, SK) — post-quantum cryptography readiness assessment ahead of the pre-announced second ECB letter (L, Medium).
7. All SEPA-scheme PSPs across the footprint, incl. RS, ME, AL, MK — SEPA scheme/EPC153-22 v2.1 migration and scheme-compliance services (L, Medium).
8. 18 Bulgarian credit institutions plus BNB and BORICA — TARGET-BNB participation, cash-cycle and post-changeover payments support (L, Medium).

| ECB Banking Supervision / SSM significant institutions | Austria, Bulgaria, Cyprus, Greece, Croatia, Slovenia, Slovakia | CONFIRMED VERBATIM AT PRIMARY TEXT. ECB letter SSM-2026-0301, signed Claudia Buch, Frankfurt am Main, 7 July 2026, 'Addressing AI-enabled cybersecurity threats', 7 pages incl. 2 annexes. Operative sentence re-read verbatim: 'This action plan should be submitted to the respective Joint Supervisory Team (JST) by 31 October 2026.' JSTs will engage on the plan and monitor progress; the ECB will run a HORIZONTAL ANALYSIS of submitted plans and share conclusions with SIs. Annex 1 lists six focus areas the plan 'should provide information on' (specific actions are offered 'for consideration', i.e. expectations rather than a prescriptive checklist): (1) prioritise protection of potential attack surfaces, incl. identification of ICT assets, third-party software and open-source components, and minimisation/continuous monitoring of internet-facing and externally exposed assets; (2) accelerate vulnerability and patch management at scale — incl. that ICT change management 'should also be reflected in contractual terms and service level agreements when an ICT service provider is involved'; (3) enhance monitoring, detection and AI-enabled defensive capabilities; (4) strengthen governance, funding, awareness training and supply chain assurance — management bodies 'should assess whether current ICT budgeting, staffing, tooling and change capacity are sufficient'; (5) reinforce defence-in-depth and cyber hygiene and 'modernising infrastructure by replacing or updating legacy, unsupported or end-of-life technologies', with Annex 1 adding that where replacement is not feasible, 'comprehensive protection through additional controls' is required, and naming 'accurate asset inventories' as a baseline control; (6) improve response/recovery and crisis management aligned with DORA. Confirmed: the annual IT Risk Questionnaire (ITRQ) collection is extended from September 2026 to FEBRUARY 2027. | ECB / ECPC (CPACE), nexo standards, Berlin Group — front-end standards for digital euro | Euro area — GR, CY, HR, SI, SK, AT, BG | CONFIRMED verbatim against the ECB's own ERPB slide deck, extracted locally. In April 2026 the ECB signed agreements with three European standardisation bodies to reuse open standards for digital euro front-end payment processing (ECB press release 24/04/2026, footnoted in the RDG sixth progress report). The short-listed standards are described by the ECB as: 'CPACE: to support contactless "tap-to-pay" payments using near-field communication between a payment device and a payment terminal'; 'nexo standards: to support services such as balance updates and reconciliation in areas of online POS, e-commerce, m-commerce and ATM digital euro payments'; 'Berlin Group: to support alias-based use cases and balance inquiries in areas like m-commerce'. The deck states these standards 'are usable immediately and will become mandatory once the legislation is adopted', and positions them on a slide against 'MasterCard, Visa, and other schemes closed protocols'. Follow-ups confirmed: 'ECB representatives are already taking part in the standardisation governing bodies' meetings' and in working groups 'e.g. ECPC Technical Working Group, Berlin Group OpenFinance Task Force'. The RDG report adds that the adopted open standards 'will be reflected in the implementation specifications of the draft rulebook' and that the rulebook's testing and certification framework 'will also reflect the necessary validation for these standards, which PSPs and technical solution providers would have to obtain for their front-end solutions.' [CPACE = Common Payment Application Contactless Extension, the European contactless terminal/card application protocol; ECPC = European Card Payment Cooperation; PSP = payment service provider.] | [ECB, 'ECB collaborates with European standard setters to facilitate digital euro payments', ERPB general technical session on the digital euro, Agenda item 2 (presentation dated 16/07/2026, published 30/07/2026); corroborated by RDG sixth progress report §4.1](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/euro/digital_euro/timeline/profuse/shared/pdf/ecb.dep260730_Item_2_ECB_Presentation_Digital_Euro_Standards.en.pdf) | 30/07/2026 | (a) WHO: every acquiring customer in the euro-area footprint, and the ATM side via the nexo 'ATM digital euro payments' line. (b) WHAT: on adoption of the Regulation, CPACE becomes a mandatory terminal kernel and nexo a mandatory acquirer/terminal messaging layer — a Verifone/Castles kernel certification plus TMS mass-deployment programme, and a nexo interface project on the NCR/x-core ATM side. Banks already running nexo-conformant acquiring are cheap to upgrade; those on proprietary or scheme-only protocols are the net-new contest. Certification is explicitly in scope: the rulebook's testing framework will require PSPs and technical solution providers to obtain validation for their front-end solutions — that is a recurring, billable certification lane for Printec. (c) WHEN TO COMMIT BUDGET: 6-12 months ahead of the Regulation's application. Open the conversation with acquirers in Q4-2026/H1-2027 as trilogues run, but sell readiness assessment, not hardware. | High that CPACE/nexo/Berlin Group remain the selected standards; timing is bound to the Regulation, not to any fixed date | Medium | XL | — | Confirm with Verifone and Castles their CPACE kernel certification roadmap and with NCR Atleos its nexo/ATM position. | European Parliament / Council — Regulation on the legal tender of euro banknotes and coins (2023/0208(COD)) | Euro area — GR, CY, HR, SI, SK, AT, BG | PARTIALLY CONFIRMED — procedure verified, substance rests on a pre-vote EP summary page. VERIFIED against the EP Legislative Observatory: ECON voted at first reading and decided to open interinstitutional negotiations on 23/06/2026; report A10-0184/2026 tabled 26/06/2026; announced in plenary 06/07/2026 (Rule 72) and CONFIRMED BY PLENARY ON 08/07/2026 — not 09/07/2026 as reported. Rapporteur Fernando Navarrete Rojas (EPP, ES), confirmed on the procedure file. The EP plenary press release of 09/07/2026 confirms the file was not contested and that euro-area countries would be obliged to keep cash accessible, businesses would not be allowed to ban cash, and regular monitoring would pay special attention to the elderly, low-income individuals and the unbanked. SUBSTANCE, sourced to the EP Legislative Train file page (last updated 20/06/2026 — i.e. BEFORE the 23/06/2026 ECON vote, so it describes the draft report, not the adopted mandate): Member States must designate national competent authorities overseeing cash ACCEPTANCE and ACCESS and submit an ANNUAL assessment to the Commission and the ECB, based on common indicators to be defined by a COMMISSION IMPLEMENTING ACT; the named indicators include 'density of cash access points in relation to the population' (access) and 'level of ex ante unilateral exclusions of payments in cash' (acceptance); where an assessment is negative the Member State must set out remedial measures such as prohibiting cash exclusions or imposing GEOGRAPHICAL ACCESS REQUIREMENTS; and if it fails to act, the COMMISSION MAY ADOPT IMPLEMENTING ACTS IMPOSING COUNTRY-SPECIFIC MEASURES. The Council's December 2025 position additionally 'effectively prohibits the refusal of cash except in narrowly defined cases' and mandates 'resilience measures to ensure the continuity of cash payments during major disruptions to electronic systems'. | [European Parliament Legislative Observatory, procedure file 2023/0208(COD), read together with the EP Legislative Train file 'Scope and effects of legal tender of euro banknotes and coins' (last updated 20/06/2026) and the EP plenary press release of 09/07/2026](https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/procedure-file?reference=2023/0208(COD)) | 08/07/2026 | POTENTIALLY THE BIGGEST ATM SIGNAL IN THIS WORKSTREAM — and note it sits in the cash file, not the digital euro file. (a) WHO: every euro-area bank in the footprint — Alpha, Eurobank, NBG, Piraeus (GR); Bank of Cyprus, Hellenic (CY); Erste&Steiermarkische, PBZ, Zagrebacka (HR); NLB, Nova KBM (SI); Slovenska sporitelna, VUB, Tatra (SK); Erste, RBI, BAWAG (AT); and Bulgarian banks post-euro. (b) WHAT, IF the density indicator survives into the adopted text: additional ATM estate in thin-density areas (NCR dispense and recycling), cash recyclers to make marginal sites economic, NCR APTRA OptiCash cash-cycle optimisation to hold cash-in-transit cost down while the estate grows, telemetry and monitoring to evidence uptime, and — from the Council's resilience clause — offline-capable dispensing during electronic-payment outages. (c) WHEN TO COMMIT BUDGET: the obligation only lands via the Commission implementing act defining the indicators, which follows adoption; banks would scope estate-density gap analyses 6-12 months ahead, i.e. from H1-2027 at the earliest. Printec can sell the gap analysis now as a pre-sales instrument — but must sell it as scenario planning, not as compliance with an adopted rule. | Medium-High that the file reaches political agreement with the package around end-2026; whether the density indicator survives into the final text is a separate and unverified question | Low | XL | — | CLOSE THE DEBT: obtain the consolidated ECON report A10-0184/2026 and confirm whether the density indicator and the Commission country-specific implementing-act backstop survive in the mandate ARTICLES rather than only in the pre-vote Legislative Train summary. Cross-read against the Slovenian post-consultation draft (consultation closed 17/08/2026) and MNB Reg 19/2025 — national mandates that already exceed this will be the first spend. |
| Magyar Nemzeti Bank (MNB) + OTP Bank, MBH Bank, K&H, Erste Bank Hungary, Raiffeisen Bank, CIB Bank, UniCredit Bank Hungary, GRANIT Bank, MagNet Bank | Hungary | CARRIED VERIFICATION DEBT CLOSED — I re-parsed the decree texts independently and BOTH halves hold exactly. (b) COUNT AND ALLOCATION: MNB Decree 19/2025 (VI.26.) Annex 1 parsed row by row — 80 settlement packages ('telepulescsomag') containing exactly 1,039 named settlements, allocated to nine payment service providers: OTP Bank Nyrt. 428 (33 packages), MBH Bank Nyrt. 195 (15), K&H Bank Zrt. 117 (9), ERSTE BANK HUNGARY Zrt. 104 (8), Raiffeisen Bank Zrt. 65 (5), CIB Bank Zrt. 52 (4), UniCredit Bank Hungary Zrt. 52 (4), GRANIT Bank Nyrt. 13 (1), MagNet Bank Zrt. 13 (1). Allocation weights (s.1): 30% debit cards with cash-withdrawal function, 25% balance-sheet total, 20% payment-service fee income, 15% retail forint sight deposits with a non-zero balance, 10% consumer payment accounts, all as at 31/12/2024; s.4(3) has MNB re-run the allocation every three years ('haromevente'), first time three years after entry into force (s.6), with an interim re-run under s.4(1) if the provider population changes. Swap/assumption contracts: for the >1,000 tranche by 20/07/2025 (ERA report by 31/07/2025); for the >500 tranche by 28/02/2026, reported into the MNB ERA system within 5 working days and by 16/03/2026 at the latest; an assumption contract may not be shorter than 5 years (s.3(2)); s.4(4) makes the newly designated provider reimburse a prior installer's documented costs incurred within 3 years. PENALTY LADDER (Act XVIII of 2025 s.2(5)–(6)): HUF 2m–200m initial fine, then on failure to cure HUF 5m at 3 months, 10m at 6, 20m at 9, 30m at 12, 50m at 15, 75m at 18, 100m at 21, 150m at 24 and 200m at 30 months. ACCESSIBILITY IS BUILT IN: NGM Decree 16/2025 s.2 requires the municipality-provided site to be easily reachable, in a high-traffic location, lit, with power and internet, and one where 'az ATM akadalymentesen elerheto' [the ATM is accessible/barrier-free] — so every one of these 1,039 machines must be an accessible install, and since all enter service after 28/06/2025 none can rely on the EAA legacy derogation. | [Nemzeti Jogszabalytar / Wolters Kluwer Jogtar — 16/2025. (V. 29.) NGM rendelet ss.1–2; 19/2025. (VI. 26.) Exact profile for NCR Atleos cash-dispense ATMs with accessibility packages, ATM telemetry and remote monitoring (no branch staff nearby to clear a jam), x-core multivendor software so a bank does not lock 400+ rural sites to one vendor stack, physical ATM security for unattended sites, and managed/field services with rural SLA cover. | European Systemic Risk Board (ESRB) | EU-wide (footprint EU-11) | CONFIRMED IN THE OFFICIAL JOURNAL, WITH ONE RESEARCHER CLAIM REMOVED AS UNSUPPORTED. Warning of the ESRB of 25 June 2026 on systemic cyber risks stemming from frontier artificial intelligence models (ESRB/2026/3), OJ C series, C/2026/3795, published 16.7.2026, ELI http://data.europa.eu/eli/C/2026/3795/oj — a formal warning under Art.3(2)(c) and Arts.16 and 18 of Regulation (EU) No 1092/2010. Recital 13 independently RECORDS the ECB deadline in OJ text: the ECB 'has requested the significant institutions to assess the impact of the evolving threat landscape without delay, and to develop by 31 October 2026 a comprehensive action plan'. Recital 6 verbatim: 'Current patching practices in the financial system are predominantly reactive, relying on periodic updates and ad hoc responses to disclosed vulnerabilities... current practices may become insufficient to maintain operational resilience.' Recital 7 warns institutions may face a choice between remaining exposed or cutting patch-testing and risking outages. The Warning identifies FOUR areas of weakened operational resilience: (a) time — ability to rapidly patch complex systems without causing operational failure, given the 'collapse of defensive time buffers'; (b) capability of defenders; (c) concentration on a few AI providers, themselves dependent on cloud, open-source and widely used software; (d) capability of authorities. Footnote 11 names the testing vehicles: 'threat intelligence-based ethical red teaming (TIBER), threat-led penetration testing (TLPT), advanced red teaming (ART) and cyber resilience stress testing (CyRST)'. The ESRB 'will reassess developments in each quarterly risk assessment at the General Board level'. NOT SUPPORTED: the claim that the ESRB escalated its assessment from 'elevated' to 'severe' — no such rating change appears anywhere in the text; the only use of 'elevated' is descriptive ('a transitional period characterised by elevated risk, high uncertainty'). That claim has been deleted. | [Official Journal of the EU, C/2026/3795 — Warning of the European Systemic Risk Board of 25 June 2026 (ESRB/2026/3)](https://www.esrb.europa.eu/pub/pdf/warnings/esrb.warning260625_on_systemic_cyber_risks_stemming_from_frontier_ai_models~ef424708cf.en.pdf) | 16/07/2026 | Gives the 31/10/2026 ECB deadline an OJ-published legal footing rather than resting on an ECB letter alone, and puts TLPT/TIBER scope and scenario content on the table for the next testing cycle. For Printec: recital 6-7 patching language is the strongest regulator-side argument for outsourcing patch-at-scale on distributed self-service estates. Sells ATM/device telemetry and monitoring (asset visibility), managed/field services for accelerated patching, physical+logical ATM security, INETCO for detection, and TLPT-scope readiness on ATM/POS channels. Macroprudential authorities in HR, SI, BG, GR, CY, RO must reflect it in their policy stance. | High — quarterly General Board reassessment is stated in the operative text | Medium | L | Medium | Track the Sept/Dec-2026 ESRB General Board quarterly risk assessments for escalation from Warning to Recommendation, which would carry 'comply or explain' force on national authorities. |
| ESAs Joint Committee (EBA, EIOPA, ESMA) | EU-wide (footprint EU-11) | CONFIRMED AT REGULATOR TIER, BUT IT IS NON-BINDING — SOFTENED ACCORDINGLY. ESA Statement JC 2026 25, 'Toward a consistent and risk-based approach for ICT risks from frontier AI models', dated 31 July 2026, 8 pages. It creates no new law: its stated goal is 'to provide context to the current situation considering the existing legislation and supervisory expectations, through possible mitigating actions that financial entities MAY implement', and its Annex states expressly that it 'does not establish additional requirements, nor should be regarded as a comprehensive checklist'. Substance confirmed: prevention 'relies on comprehensive and continuously updated inventories of all IT assets (including infrastructure, applications, data repositories, APIs, and AI/ML components)' enabling classification by criticality and exposure; 'Monitoring processes transition from periodic to continuous'; 'Apply continuous vulnerability scanning and monitoring' because periodic scanning 'may no longer be sufficient'; and it cites 'Article 10 of the RTS RMF' (the regulatory technical standards on the ICT risk-management framework) as the basis for measures ranging from basic automated vulnerability scanning and patch management up to AI-augmented DevSecOps. Para 10 confirmed verbatim in substance: the ESAs AS LEAD OVERSEERS 'initiated targeted engagement with relevant CTPPs' (critical ICT third-party providers), the insights 'informed the annual risk assessment cycle and the prioritisation of activities under the 2027 Oversight Plan', they 'have also begun embedding AI-related risks into its Oversight Examination Methodology and will continue to strengthen this work throughout 2027', and these threats are expected to be reflected in the scope of 2027 oversight examinations. It builds on the European Commission's Action Plan on Cybersecurity and Artificial Intelligence published 7 July 2026 and on ENISA's frontier-AI recommendations. NOT CHECKED THIS RUN: the assertion that no second CTPP designation round was announced and that the 19-provider list of 18/11/2025 stands — treat as unverified. | [EBA/EIOPA/ESMA Joint Committee — JC 2026 25, ESA Statement on frontier AI models (PDF, 8pp)](https://www.esma.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2026-07/JC_2026_25_ESA_statement_on_frontier_AI_models.pdf) | 31/07/2026 | Extends the ECB's SI-only 31/10/2026 push into supervisory dialogue with ALL DORA financial entities — including less-significant banks and payment institutions in RO, HU, CZ and other non-SSM parts of the footprint — but as expectation, not deadline, so it is a door-opener rather than a forced buy. The continuously-updated IT asset inventory covering infrastructure and APIs is precisely the documented gap in most banks' ATM/POS estates: sells ATM/device telemetry and monitoring, TMS for POS terminal estates (Verifone/Castles), x-core for multivendor ATM software visibility, and managed services that deliver continuous rather than periodic monitoring. | High as supervisory dialogue — national competent authorities are expected to align; but no dated obligation attaches | Medium | L | Medium | Watch for the ESAs' 2027 Oversight Plan (expected late 2026) and any second CTPP designation round; check whether BNR, MNB and CNB-CZ issue matching national circulars. Separately verify the CTPP designation list status, which was not re-checked here. |
| ESAs / national competent authorities — threat-led penetration testing (TLPT) | Croatia, Greece, Cyprus, Bulgaria, Romania, Austria, Slovenia, Slovakia, Czech Republic, Hungary | LEGAL BASIS CONFIRMED AT TWO REGULATOR SOURCES. TLPT — threat-led penetration testing, a controlled adversary-simulation red-team exercise run against live production systems, mandated by DORA Articles 26-27 — is specified by Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2025/1190 of 13 February 2025, made under DORA Art.26(11). Both ESMA's DORA Level-2 page and the Croatian National Bank's DORA page carry the full title. HNB, in Croatian: 'Delegirana uredba Komisije 2025/1190 оd 13. veljače 2025. ... kojima se utvrđuju kriteriji za određivanje koji su financijski subjekti dužni provoditi penetracijsko testiranje vođeno prijetnjama, zahtjevi i standardi koji se primjenjuju na angažiranje unutarnjih provoditelja testiranja, zahtjevi povezani s opsegom i metodologijom testiranja i pristupom svakoj pojedinačnoj fazi testiranja, rezultatima, završetkom testiranja i fazama testiranja koje se odnose na ispravljanje nedostataka te vrsta nadzorne suradnje ... potrebni za provedbu TLPT-a i olakšavanje njegova uzajamnog priznavanja' [the criteria for determining which financial entities must carry out threat-led penetration testing, the requirements and standards for the use of INTERNAL testers, requirements on scope, testing methodology and the approach to each test phase, results, closure and the remediation phases, and the type of supervisory cooperation needed to implement TLPT and facilitate its MUTUAL RECOGNITION]. ESRB Warning ESRB/2026/3 footnote 11 (verified above) points authorities to TIBER, TLPT, ART and CyRST as the levers for closing the frontier-AI defender gap, which makes expanded TLPT scope and scenario content a likely next supervisory move. NOTE ON PRECISION: the three-year minimum testing cycle derives from DORA Art.26(1) itself and was not re-read this run; and neither the RTS title nor HNB says tests are 'executed under the TIBER-EU framework' — TIBER-EU is the ECB's aligned framework, not a requirement named in 2025/1190. Both points are softened here. | [Hrvatska narodna banka — 'Digitalna operativna otpornost' (DORA framework page listing Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2025/1190); independently corroborated on ESMA's DORA Level-2 page](https://www.hnb.hr/-/digitalna-operativna-otpornost) | 13/02/2025 | TLPT scope is set at entity level by the competent authority and covers critical/important functions END TO END — for a retail bank that pulls in the ATM channel, the card-acquiring and POS estate, and the terminal-management and monitoring layer. Printec operates parts of that scope (x-core, TMS, telemetry, managed/field services) and must be contractually ready to support a client's red-team window, including the disclosure and cooperation terms the RTS contemplates. Sells: physical and logical ATM security hardening, INETCO transaction monitoring for detection coverage during the test, and remediation work after the test report. Mutual recognition means a group-level test at Erste, RBI, OTP, NLB or UniCredit can pull Printec-operated estates in several footprint countries into a single test scope at once. | High for designated entities; footprint SSM banks are the likeliest first cohort | Medium | L | Medium | Ask HNB, BNB, BoG, CBC and BNR which entities they have designated for TLPT and in which testing window; confirm whether Printec's contracts with designated banks contain the cooperation and disclosure clauses TLPT requires. The closing paragraph of ECB letter SSM-2026-0301 (07/07/2026) reads: 'the ECB would like to highlight that other emerging technologies, like the ongoing progress towards practical quantum computing, will have a significant impact on the cybersecurity landscape. | Bulgarian PSPs (all credit institutions, EMIs and payment institutions) — Instant Payments Regulation | Bulgaria | CARRIED VERIFICATION DEBT CLOSED AGAINST THE OFFICIAL JOURNAL TEXT. Regulation (EU) No 260/2012 Article 16, new paragraph 9, inserted by Article 1(5) of Regulation (EU) 2024/886 (the Instant Payments Regulation), reads verbatim: 'If the euro is introduced as the currency of a Member State before 9 April 2027, the PSPs in that Member State shall comply with Articles 5a, 5b and 5c within one year of the date on which the euro has been introduced as the currency of that Member State and not later than the respective dates specified in those Articles for PSPs in Member States whose currency is not the euro. However, such PSPs shall not be required to comply with Articles 5a, 5b and 5c earlier than the respective dates specified in those Articles for PSPs in the Member States whose currency is the euro.' Bulgaria introduced the euro on 01/01/2026, before 09/04/2027, so the one-year rule bites and Bulgarian PSPs must comply BY 01/01/2027 with Art.5a (offering BOTH receiving AND sending of instant credit transfers in euro), Art.5b (instant-transfer charges no higher than for equivalent non-instant transfers, and the Art.5c verification services provided FREE OF CHARGE) and Art.5c (VERIFICATION OF PAYEE — VoP, the check that the payee name matches the IBAN, performed immediately after the payer provides payee details and BEFORE the payer is offered the possibility of authorising, on EVERY payment initiation channel). All euro-area floor dates (5a receiving 09/01/2025 and sending 09/10/2025; 5b 09/01/2025; 5c 09/10/2025) are already past, so the floor does not delay Bulgaria. Recital 11 of Regulation (EU) 2024/886 names the channels expressly: 'online banking, a mobile application, AN AUTOMATED TELLER MACHINE, A SELF-SERVICE TERMINAL, in a branch or by phone', and requires that 'there should be no difference in terms of the payment initiation channels' between instant and other credit transfers — the ATM/kiosk estate is inside scope as a matter of primary EU text, not inference, and ZVERB §1 item 16 defines a Bulgarian ATM as including transfers between payment accounts. Art.5d (daily screening of PSUs against targeted financial restrictive measures instead of per-transaction screening) is NOT listed in Art.16(9) and carried its own single deadline of 09/01/2025 for all PSPs. | [Regulation (EU) 2024/886 amending Regulation (EU) No 260/2012, Art.1(5) inserting Art.16(9), plus Arts.5a(8), 5b(3), 5c(9) and recital 11 — Official Journal text (OJ L, 2024/886, 19.3.2024) via the EU Publications Office CELEX resource](http://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/32024R0886) | 01/01/2027 | Every Bulgarian PSP has a hard, directly applicable EU-law go-live on 01/01/2027 — four months out — covering sending instant euro transfers, instant-transfer pricing parity, and a full Verification-of-Payee service on EVERY initiation channel, with recital 11 naming ATMs and self-service terminals explicitly. | PSR Article 50 + Article 57 - Verification of Payee extended beyond the euro instant-payments perimeter | EU footprint, sharpest effect in non-euro markets: CZ, HU, RO, BG; also HR/GR/CY/SI/SK/AT | CONFIRMED VERBATIM, WITH TWO SCOPE QUALIFIERS THE RESEARCHER MISSED. Confirmed at ST 8221/26 p.277: PSR Art.50 requires that 'In the case of credit transfers, payment service providers shall comply with provisions of Articles 5c(1) to (7) and 5b(2) of Regulation (EU) 260/2012, and those articles shall apply mutatis mutandis to all credit transfers, including those that fall outside the scope of Regulation (EU) 260/2012' - Reg. 260/2012 being the SEPA Regulation as amended by the Instant Payments Regulation (EU) 2024/886. Where the payee account is not identified by an IBAN, references to the payment account identifier are read as 'the unique identifier used to unambiguously identify the payment account of the payee'. Art.57 confirmed at p.291: where a PSP fails to comply with Art.50 and that failure results in a defectively executed transaction, the payer's PSP 'shall without delay refund the payer the amount transferred' and restore the debited account; where the fault lies with the payee's PSP or the payment initiation service provider, that party 'shall compensate the payer's PSP for the financial damage'. Both apply at EIF+27m per Art.112. QUALIFIER 1 (recital 71, confirmed verbatim at p.63): the Art.50 service 'should only apply to credit transfers which are not covered by Regulation (EU) 2024/886' - Art.50 is the RESIDUAL layer on top of the instant-payments VoP, not a duplicate of it. This strengthens rather than weakens the Printec angle: the delta is precisely the domestic-currency and non-IBAN traffic. QUALIFIER 2 (confirmed at pp.425-427): the PSR amends Reg. 260/2012 Art.5c(6) so that payment service users who are NOT consumers can OPT OUT of the verification service when submitting payment orders via automated dedicated processes or protocols made available only to non-consumers - i.e. corporate bulk/host-to-host files. That materially narrows corporate VoP volumes and must be modelled before sizing. Recital 70 additionally confirms that for legal-person payees the match may be run against a fiscal number, a European unique identifier or an LEI rather than a name. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8221/26 (PSR final compromise, LIMITE), Articles 50, 57, 112, recitals 70-73, and the amendments to Regulation (EU) No 260/2012](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: banks in CZ, HU, RO and BG that are building VoP only for euro SCT / SCT Inst under the Instant Payments Regulation, plus every footprint bank running domestic-currency or non-IBAN proxy/alias credit transfers. PRINTEC LANE: INETCO transaction monitoring and payment-data tooling as the evidence layer; the extension is a re-scope of an existing build, not a second product. BUDGET COMMIT: 12-18 months before EIF+27m, i.e. immediately after the IPR VoP build so it is reused rather than re-bought. | PSR Article 59 - impersonation ('spoofing') fraud: standalone prevention duty plus 15-business-day refund clock | EU footprint (all 10 EU markets) | CONFIRMED VERBATIM at ST 8221/26 pp.293-294. Art.59(-1) is a standalone PREVENTION obligation: PSPs 'shall have adequate prevention and robust technical safeguards in place to prevent cases where fraudsters replicate and misuse the payment service provider's communication channels for misleading payment service users into making fraudulent transactions.' Art.59(1) requires a FULL refund where a consumer was manipulated by a third party pretending to be the consumer's PSP 'using communication channels attributed to the consumer's payment service provider', conditional on the consumer notifying the PSP without undue delay after becoming aware and reporting the fraud to the police. Art.59(2) sets a hard clock: 'Within 15 business days of being notified and provided with the police report by the consumer', the PSP must either refund or, where it has objectively justified reasons to suspect consumer fraud or gross negligence, provide a justification for refusing and indicate the ADR/complaint bodies under Arts.90, 91, 93, 94 and 95. Art.59(3) disapplies the refund where the consumer acted fraudulently or with gross negligence; Art.59(4) puts the burden of proof on the PSP to establish that, requires the PSP to invite the consumer's account of events and factor it into the assessment, and provides that a consumer's failure to supply that information 'shall not in itself lead the payment service provider to conclude that the consumer acted fraudulently or with gross negligence', and that the consumer 'shall not be expected to provide information beyond what such a consumer can reasonably be expected to have'. VERIFIER CORRECTION: the researcher dated the compromise publication 23/04/2026; the primary document is dated 17/04/2026 and no 23/04/2026 publication step could be verified - that date has been struck. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8221/26 (PSR final compromise, LIMITE), Article 59](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: every PSP serving consumers in the 10 EU footprint markets. WHAT THEY MUST BUY OR CHANGE, three distinct builds: (a) anti-spoofing controls over the bank's own OUTBOUND channels - SMS sender-ID protection, caller-ID integrity, e-mail authentication - a prevention duty that no PSD2 control satisfies today; (b) a disputes/claims workflow that provably meets 15 business days from notification-plus-police-report, with reasoned refusals that name the ADR bodies; (c) evidence capture strong enough to discharge a reversed burden of proof, including a logged invitation to the consumer to give their account. PRINTEC LANE: INETCO real-time transaction monitoring, FICO and IMTF Siron for behavioural/scam detection and case management, Namirial eKYC for identity assurance inside the claims flow. BUDGET COMMIT: 9-12 months before EIF+21m. | High - the Art.59 text is agreed in the confirmed compromise; only the calendar date and final numbering are open | Medium | L | — | Model the 15-business-day SLA against each customer's current disputes platform and measure today's actual cycle time. Identify which footprint banks have no outbound-channel spoofing control (SMS sender-ID, caller-ID, DMARC) at all - that is the Art.59(-1) target list. |

(c) WHEN TO COMMIT BUDGET: NOT YET. Because the ECB has explicitly deprioritised ATM use cases and no ATM certification obligation exists, there is no 2026-27 CAPEX trigger. | ECB / European Payments Council (EPC) — fourth standardisation agreement (MoU) | Euro area + SEPA footprint | CONFIRMED, but SOURCE CORRECTED. A fourth standardisation agreement with the European Payments Council is being formalised through a Memorandum of Understanding. The ERPB deck states verbatim: 'These discussions have also laid the groundwork for a productive and sustainable partnership between the ECB and the EPC to be formalised through a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU). The process for approving the MoU by the ECB decision-making bodies was initiated at the end of June 2026.' The RDG sixth progress report §4.1 independently states only that 'a similar agreement is envisaged with the European Payments Council (EPC)' and names the candidate standards verbatim: 'One key example is the standard for QR-code payments (EPC024-22), which could be used for online POS, e-commerce, m-commerce and, potentially, ATM transactions using QR codes. Technical investigations are underway to explore the potential reuse of other EPC standards, such as Verification of Payee.' [EPC = European Payments Council, the SEPA scheme-management body; EPC024-22 = the EPC's SEPA QR-code standard; VoP = Verification of Payee, the check that a payee's name matches the IBAN before a transfer is executed; MoU = memorandum of understanding, a non-binding cooperation instrument.] | [ECB, 'ECB collaborates with European standard setters to facilitate digital euro payments', ERPB Agenda item 2, slide 7 (presentation 16/07/2026, published 30/07/2026); EPC024-22 and VoP wording from RDG sixth progress report §4.1 (06/07/2026)](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/euro/digital_euro/timeline/profuse/shared/pdf/ecb.dep260730_Item_2_ECB_Presentation_Digital_Euro_Standards.en.pdf) | 30/07/2026 | (a) WHO: euro-area ATM deployers in the footprint plus acquirers on the POS side. (b) WHAT: EPC024-22 QR is the second initiation route already written into rulebook Annex D1 #4 alongside card. If the MoU lands and EPC024-22 is referenced normatively, QR becomes a formal digital euro acceptance channel — ATMs need a QR scanner or on-screen dynamic-QR capability (a fascia/peripheral question on much of the installed NCR base, a pure software question on newer machines) and POS terminals need a QR path. VoP reuse is the cross-sell: the same VoP component being funded in CZ/HU/RO for the 09/07/2027 instant-payments obligation could be reused, making one funded build serve two mandates. (c) WHEN TO COMMIT BUDGET: no commit yet — the MoU is not signed and EPC024-22 is not normative in the rulebook. Run the QR-capability survey of the installed base now as a pre-sales asset so the bank has the gap analysis when the trigger comes. | Medium-High that the MoU is signed within 6 months; the approval process was initiated end-June 2026 | Medium | L | — | Watch for an ECB press release announcing the EPC MoU, then check whether EPC024-22 is referenced normatively in the next rulebook version's ATM section. Next steps stated verbatim: 'Finalisation of a structured proposal for follow-up analyses of issues emerging from the 30 June workshop – topical separations between the RDG and ERPB to be communicated to the market' and 'Kickstart collaborative work on mapping out the digital euro rollout strategy. The April 2026 co-badging deep-dive concluded co-badging is a voluntary opportunity that 'could let digital euro work alongside existing private payment solutions, give domestic card schemes a pan-euro area network, and reduce reliance on international schemes within the euro area', with a footnote that 'legal provisions may have to cater for it (e.g. interplay with IFR)'. [ERPB = Euro Retail Payments Board, the ECB-chaired forum of payment supply and demand side representatives; IFR = Interchange Fee Regulation; co-badging = carrying two payment brands on one card or wallet.] | [ECB, 'Fit in the Ecosystem — stocktake and next steps', ERPB general technical session on the digital euro, Agenda item 4 (presentation 16/07/2026, published 30/07/2026; PDF re-fetched and text-extracted independently)](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/euro/digital_euro/timeline/profuse/shared/pdf/ecb.dep260730_Item_4_ECB_Presentation_Fit_in_the_Ecosystem_Stocktake.en.pdf) | 30/07/2026 | 13/10/2026 is the next hard, dated event in this workstream and the first time the ECB will discuss a ROLLOUT STRATEGY with the market — the eventual home of the acceptance-readiness dates Printec's customers need for budgeting, and therefore the single calendar entry to hold. 'Offline: payment acceptance and readiness requirements' sitting on the future-analysis list is the thread that eventually reaches the ATM and terminal estate, since offline acceptance needs secure elements and HSM key management (the Thales lane). | Ministry of Finance (Ministrstvo za finance) / Banka Slovenije — draft ZDUPS (Act on Accessibility and Use of Payment Instruments) | Slovenia | OUTCOME OF THE 17/08/2026 CONSULTATION (re-verified word-for-word on the MoF release). The MoF closed the public consultation on the draft Zakon o dostopnosti in uporabi placilnih sredstev ('ZDUPS') having received 23 written responses ('23 odzivov') from individuals and legal persons, expert bodies, interest associations, municipalities and their associations, ministries and other affected bodies. The MoF release records that respondents proposed 'drugacni oziroma novi kriteriji za mrezo gotovinskih tock' [different or new criteria for the cash-point network], plus: wider exemptions from mandatory cash acceptance; different rules on free euro banknote/coin withdrawals and deposits at branches; complete removal of the mandatory e-payment-acceptance obligation into a separate legislative procedure (others asked only for wider exemptions and a clearer definition of an electronic payment instrument); extension of the free basic-payment-services package to people without regular income (e.g. farmers); constitutionality warnings on that package; and 'daljse obdobje za prilagoditev zakonskim zahtevam in za dopolnitev prekrskovnih dolocb' [a longer adaptation period and supplementing of the penalty provisions]. The MoF will review the comments, consult Banka Slovenije, and states 'Pricakujemo tudi se mnenje Evropske centralne banke' [we also still expect the opinion of the European Central Bank] — an ECB Art.127(4)/282(5) TFEU consultation is therefore still pending. The bill regulates the 'ustavno priznano pravico do uporabe gotovine' [constitutionally recognised right to use cash]. NO adopted text, NO gazette date, NO in-force date as at 27/08/2026, so the cash-point network criteria are formally contested and unsettled. IMPORTANT CORRECTION: the specific figures previously reported — a 60 ATMs/100,000-inhabitants density floor and a 5 km / >1,000-inhabitant settlement rule — do NOT appear in the MoF release and could NOT be verified against the draft bill text in this pass; treat them as unverified until the e-uprava draft is obtained. The end-2026 adoption target is PRESS-TIER ONLY ('Cilj je, da bi bil zakon sprejet do konca leta' — STA via RTV SLO, 17/08/2026), not an MoF-primary statement; the draft went to consultation on 17/07/2026 and an earlier similar draft was published in March 2026. | [Ministrstvo za finance / GOV.SI — 'Zakljucek javne obravnave predloga Zakona o dostopnosti in uporabi placilnih sredstev' (primary); end-2026 target and consultation start date: STA via RTV SLO/MMC, 17/08/2026 (press-tier)](https://www.gov.si/novice/2026-08-17-zakljucek-javne-obravnave-predloga-zakona-o-dostopnosti-in-uporabi-placilnih-sredstev/) | 17/08/2026 | Largest ATM/self-service capex trigger in the SI market, still live and UNPRICED. | Deputy Ministry of Social Welfare (Yfypourgeio Koinonikis Pronoias) / Council of Ministers — Cyprus EAA transposition | Cyprus | [STANDING — dated 2024] CARRIED DEBT CLOSED against the primary legal text, which I extracted and read myself. Cyprus transposed the European Accessibility Act (Directive (EU) 2019/882) by the Accessibility of Products and Services Law of 2024, N.57(I)/2024, published in the Official Gazette of the Republic of Cyprus, Annex I(I), No. 4996 of Thursday 2 May 2024. Verified in the gazette text: (i) s.3(1) — the COMPETENT AUTHORITY ('armodia archi') is the Deputy Minister of Social Welfare, NOT the Central Bank of Cyprus and NOT the Consumer Protection Service; s.3(2) lets him delegate to officers of his Deputy Ministry; (ii) s.3(3) — the Council of Ministers designates the public departments and services that co-operate with the competent authority and act as 'arches epitirisis tis prosvasimotitas' [accessibility surveillance authorities]; I found NO published Council of Ministers designation decision, so the operational market-surveillance layer remains a live gap; s.3(4) requires co-operation with the Cyprus Confederation of Organisations of the Disabled (KYSOA); (iii) s.4(1) — the Law applies to products PLACED ON THE MARKET FROM 28/06/2025, expressly including self-service terminals: payment terminals, and 'aftomates tameiakes michanes' [ATMs], ticketing machines, check-in machines and interactive information terminals; (iv) s.20(1) applies a named subset of Regulation (EU) 2019/1020 (the EU market-surveillance regulation) to product surveillance; (v) s.36(1) — ADMINISTRATIVE FINE up to EUR 10,000, up to EUR 20,000 for a subsequent breach, with a continuing-breach uplift in s.36(2); the CRIMINAL offence provision is s.38 (s.35 is the administrative-offence provision that feeds s.36): on first conviction up to 2 years' imprisonment and/or EUR 20,000, on second or subsequent conviction up to 3 years and/or EUR 30,000; (vi) s.45(1) — transitional period ending 28/06/2030 during which service providers may continue providing services using products they lawfully used before that date, with service contracts agreed before 28/06/2025 able to run to expiry but never beyond five years from that date. | [CyLaw — N.57(I)/2024, 'O peri tis Prosvasimotitas Proionton kai Ypiresion Nomos tou 2024' [Accessibility of Products and Services Law of 2024], Official Gazette Annex I(I) No. 4996, 02/05/2024 (PDF re-downloaded and text re-extracted locally with pdfminer)](https://www.cylaw.org/nomoi/arith/2024_1_057.pdf) | 02/05/2024 [STANDING — dated 2024] | Bank of Cyprus, Hellenic Bank, Eurobank Cyprus, Alpha Bank Cyprus, Astrobank and the co-op successors face a hard 28/06/2030 cliff for every ATM and payment terminal lawfully in service before 28/06/2025, and an immediate obligation on anything placed on the market from 28/06/2025. Printec sells directly into this: NCR Atleos accessible ATMs (audio jack, tactile keypad, screen-reader/voice guidance, reach and contrast per EN 301 549), retrofit accessibility kits where the chassis allows, x-core multivendor ATM software to deliver one compliant accessible UI across a mixed estate without full hardware replacement, and accessible Verifone/Castles PIN-entry devices with TMS to push compliant terminal software. | Hungarian Parliament / MNB — Act XIV of 2026 amendment to the ATM Act (2025. evi XVIII. torveny) | Hungary | IN FORCE 14/05/2026 — ATM DECOMMISSIONING AND RELOCATION FREEZE, re-verified against the consolidated statute. Act XVIII of 2025 on the installation of ATMs (promulgated 12/05/2025) was amended by Act XIV of 2026 s.96(1)–(2): s.3(1)(a) was rewritten and new subsections (1a), (1b) and (1c) inserted, all in force from 14/05/2026. s.3(1): a credit institution operating an ATM may not cease operating an ATM already in existence when the Act entered into force — 'ide nem ertve az ATM csereje' [not counting replacement of the ATM] — and s.3(1)(b) applies the same no-closure rule to a branch's cash-payout service from a payment account. (1a): it may change the site of an existing ATM only in a justified case and only if, after removal, it continues to operate an ATM within the same settlement or Budapest district. (1b): 'Ha az ATM csereje egy adott helyszinen kizarolag technologiai, muszaki okok miatt - igy kulonosen a kihelyezendo uj ATM meretei, vedelmi kovetelmenyei miatt - nem valosithato meg, akkor a csere ATM kizarolag az erintett telepulesen, fovarosi keruleten belul telepitheto' [if replacing the ATM at a given site cannot be done for purely technological or technical reasons — in particular the DIMENSIONS of the new ATM to be deployed and its SECURITY REQUIREMENTS — the replacement ATM may only be installed within the same settlement or district]. (1c): justified cases include in particular deployment in a settlement with no ATM, justification on average cash-withdrawal volume data within the settlement/district, or landlord termination of the site lease. Breach of s.3(1) carries an MNB fine of HUF 2m–200m (s.3(2)). | [Nemzeti Jogszabalytar — 2025. evi XVIII. torveny az automata bankjegykiado gepek telepiteserol, consolidated text as at 14/05/2026, amendment notes citing '2026. evi XIV. torveny 96. § (1)' and '(2)', 'Hatalyos: 2026. V. 14-tol'; cross-checked on Wolters Kluwer Jogtar at https://net.jogtar.hu/jogszabaly?docid=a2500018.tv](https://njt.jog.gov.hu/jogszabaly/2025-18-00-00) | 14/05/2026 | An unusually direct hardware signal. Hungarian banks are legally locked into their existing ATM footprint and may only REPLACE, never retire — so the OTP/MBH/K&H/Erste/Raiffeisen/CIB/UniCredit/Granit/MagNet estate becomes a guaranteed refresh market rather than a shrinking one. Subsection (1b) expressly anticipates that a replacement machine may not physically fit the old site because of its DIMENSIONS and SECURITY REQUIREMENTS — precisely what happens when a legacy through-the-wall dispenser is swapped for a deeper intelligent-deposit or recycling ATM, or for a machine carrying EAA accessibility hardware. Printec should lead with NCR Atleos recyclers plus a chargeable site-survey/fit study, x-core to hold the software layer constant across old and new hardware, and physical ATM security to satisfy the 'vedelmi kovetelmenyei' limb. Subsection (1c)(b) also creates demand for ATM telemetry, since a bank needs average withdrawal-volume evidence to justify any move. | In force now; every Hungarian ATM refresh decision from 14/05/2026 onward runs through these rules. | Medium | L | — | Obtain 2026. evi XIV. torveny s.96 in Magyar Kozlony to confirm the promulgation date and read the explanatory memorandum (not done this run); ask MNB whether a replacement that fails (1b) requires prior notification. |

Bulgaria's EAA transposition is the Zakon za iziskvaniyata za dostapnost na produkti i uslugi [Law on Accessibility Requirements for Products and Services], adopted by the 51st National Assembly on 28/03/2025, promulgated by Presidential Decree No. 58 and published in Darzhaven vestnik issue 31 of 11/04/2025, in force 28/06/2025. Verified in the gazette text: (i) Art.2 covers self-service terminals — 'platezhni terminali' [payment terminals] and 'terminalni ustroystva ATM' [ATM terminal devices] — alongside ticketing machines, passenger check-in terminals and interactive information terminals, and covers consumer banking services; (ii) Art.34(1) makes the CHAIRMAN OF DAMTN, through the agency's market-surveillance bodies, the market-surveillance authority for those products, applying Regulation (EU) 2019/1020; Art.39 lets him order temporary suspension of making a product available, a PROHIBITION on making it available or putting it into service, or WITHDRAWAL of the product; (iii) Art.54(1)(8) makes the BNB the control body for consumer banking services, with the Financial Supervision Commission for investment services and the Consumer Protection Commission for e-commerce; (iv) transitional provision para.3 halved all Chapter Six fines and property sanctions for infringements committed 'do 1 yuni 2026 g.' [up to 1 June 2026], so from 01/06/2026 the FULL rates apply: Art.73(1) base fine BGN 1,000–5,000 on individuals or property sanction BGN 5,000–25,000 on undertakings, doubling to BGN 2,000–10,000 / BGN 10,000–50,000 on repeat breach; (v) para.4(3) — self-service terminals lawfully used by service providers before 28/06/2025 may run to the end of their economic life but no longer than 20 years from entry into use; para.4(1)–(2) give the 28/06/2030 service-provider transition and the five-year cap on pre-28/06/2025 service contracts. BASELINE CORRECTION CONFIRMED: the '28/06/2027 BG EAA national supervisory milestone' attributed to DAMTN is NOT an ATM supervisory milestone — para.9(1) delays only para.5 (amendments to the Law on the National 112 Emergency Call System, on handling calls from people with hearing or speech impairments) to 28/06/2027, and para.4(4) allows the Annex 2 Section IV(a) emergency-call obligations to be met via a relay service provider until the same date. Neither touches ATMs or payment terminals. | [Darzhaven vestnik (State Gazette of the Republic of Bulgaria), issue 31 of 11/04/2025 — Zakon za iziskvaniyata za dostapnost na produkti i uslugi, full official text (re-fetched and grep-verified)](https://dv.parliament.bg/DVWeb/showMaterialDV.jsp?idMat=233836) | 01/06/2026 (fine-discount expiry); law promulgated 11/04/2025 | Two distinct levers. Art.33 mirrors this for authorised representatives; Art.36(1) applies the same EUR 6,630–132,720 band to a SERVICE PROVIDER that fails to make the design and provision of its service accessible, fails to prepare the Annex V information, or fails to co-operate with the competent inspector under Art.25; | Central Bank of the Republic of Kosovo (CBK / BQK) | Kosovo | FULLY CONFIRMED FROM THE 48-PAGE PRIMARY TEXT, AND STRONGER THAN THE RESEARCHER STATED. The CBK Regulation on Information Systems and Cyber Risk Management was approved by the Board of the Central Bank of the Republic of Kosovo at its meeting held on 29 August 2025. Article 67 verbatim: 'This Regulation enters into force on 15 September 2025. Financial institutions - FIs are obliged to comply with the requirements of this Regulation, from 1 June 2026.' That compliance date has ALREADY PASSED. It is a 48-page DORA-shaped rulebook applying to banking and non-banking financial institutions, built on a proportionality principle, with chapters on governance and organisation; strategy, policies and procedures; ICT ASSET AND INFORMATION MANAGEMENT; MANAGEMENT OF THIRD-PARTY SERVICE PROVIDERS; BUDGET FORECAST; technology and cyber risk management; project management and IT acquisition; system development lifecycle and security by design; testing; patch deployment; access rights; and cloud services. It EXCEEDS the EU minimum on incident timing — Art.31(10): 'The FI must notify and report the incident to the CBK, no later than 4 hours after its discovery', with 'An interim report... within 72 hours and the final report within 30 days', and the Annex 1 template demands hardware manufacturer, software developer and make/model of affected applications and of the systems they run on. Article 10 (Budget forecast) is the commercially useful one and is sharper than reported: 'FIs should allocate sufficient budgetary funds to meet the appropriate level of cyber preparedness' AND 'The cybersecurity budget should be independent of the FI's overall ICT budget, to ensure that business-related systems developments do not compete for resources allocated to protecting ICT systems.' Cloud use for critical services requires board approval, a register of all cloud services must be available at all times, and CBK must be notified one month before engaging a cloud provider for critical services. Article 66 repeals the old Regulation on Information Technology for Banks and related provisions. | [Central Bank of the Republic of Kosovo — 'Regulation on Information Systems and Cyber Risk Management' (English text, 48pp PDF)](https://bqk-kos.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/ENG-Rregullore-per-sistemet-e-informacionit-1-1.pdf?lang=en) | 01/06/2026 | Kosovo banks (BKT Kosova, Raiffeisen Bank Kosovo, ProCredit Bank Kosovo, NLB Banka Kosovo, TEB, Banka Ekonomike, Banka për Biznes) are non-compliant today if they cannot produce an ICT asset register, a third-party provider register, a documented patch process and a 4-hour incident pipeline. The ring-fenced cybersecurity budget in Art.10 is the single most useful clause for Printec anywhere in this set: it forces a board-visible spend line that business projects cannot cannibalise. Directly saleable: ATM/device telemetry and monitoring (the asset register plus the make/model/firmware evidence Annex 1 demands), x-core for multivendor estate visibility, managed/field services for documented patch deployment, physical ATM security. WHEN BUDGET COMMITS: immediately — the obligation is already live and the next step is inspection. Kosovo has no firm SEPA date, so this, not payments, is the near-term Kosovo spend trigger. | High — binding since 01/06/2026; supervisory enforcement is the next step | Medium | M | Medium | Ask CBK's supervision department whether on-site inspections against the regulation have begun and whether any enforcement has issued; pitch a Kosovo-wide 'ICT asset register + 4-hour incident evidence' bundle built on device telemetry, and lead the commercial conversation with the Art.10 ring-fenced cybersecurity budget. |

CONFIRMED: new Art.3a 'Protection of the portrait' - wallet providers must ensure wallet solutions warn the user that a request involves sharing BIOMETRIC data when the portrait is requested and take explicit confirmation ('Silence or pre-ticked boxes should not be considered as confirmation'); new Art.14a 'EU Digital Identity Wallet Trust Mark' with reference colours Pantone No 661 and 116 and a minimum size of 64 x 85 pixels at 150 dpi; new Annex Ia (mandatory cryptographic mechanisms, via new Art.5a) and Annex Ib (wallet unit attestation / WUA technical requirements); CTAP (Client To Authenticator Protocol) version 2.3 local communication channel preferred by gatekeepers over CTAP Hybrid tunnel services in cross-device flows; PID encoding updated in ISO/IEC mdoc and SD-JWT VC with W3C VCDM support once profiles exist. TWO CORRECTIONS: (1) the original signal reported ONE 11/08/2028 date. There are TWO. Recital 3 defers the portrait as mandatory PID to 11/08/2028 - but recital 6 ALSO defers to 11/08/2028 the requirement for wallet units to AUTHENTICATE AND VALIDATE WALLET-RELYING-PARTY REGISTRATION CERTIFICATES, which materially relaxes near-term RP pressure. (2) the portrait is not unconditionally mandatory from 11/08/2028: the amended Annex states 'Member States may provide that the user has the option to decline the insertion of the portrait to the person identification data'. | [EU Publications Office (CELLAR), official OJ text of Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1731, CELEX 32026R1731](http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg_impl/2026/1731/oj) | 15/07/2026 | Two build items for Printec's eKYC/onboarding line: (a) the biometric-portrait warning plus explicit-confirmation flow - a UX and DPIA change in any bank flow that requests a portrait from a wallet; (b) trust-mark and wallet-unit-attestation verification logic on the RP side. Because the portrait is not guaranteed in the PID before 11/08/2028 (and even then a Member State may let users decline it), banks CANNOT assume a wallet will supply a portrait - document-plus-selfie remote identity proofing (Namirial) remains necessary ALONGSIDE wallet acceptance, not replaced by it. The second 11/08/2028 deferral (wallet-side validation of RP registration certificates) means the RP registration-certificate leg is a 2027-2028 build, not a 2026 emergency. | European Commission - Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1730 (amends the wallet-relying-party registration act) | EU (AT,BG,HR,CY,CZ,GR,HU,RO,SK,SI) | NEW ROW ADDED BY THE VERIFIER - this act was named in the ARF 3.0.0 release notes but was entirely absent from the researcher's set. Re-verified against the official OJ text (CELLAR, CELEX 32026R1730): 'COMMISSION IMPLEMENTING REGULATION (EU) 2026/1730 of 15 July 2026 amending Implementing Regulation (EU) 2025/848 as regards applicable standards and specifications', OJ 22.7.2026, in force on the twentieth day after publication (= 11/08/2026). It rewrites the RP registration machinery: (a) Art.6(3)(a) is narrowed so registrars verify accuracy/validity/authenticity/integrity only of Annex I points 1-6 and 11-16, and Art.6(3)(c) is deleted; (b) a new Art.6(3a) requires registrars to collect Annex I points 7-10 (the entitlement / requested-attribute data) IN AN AUTOMATED MANNER ONLY FOR TRANSPARENCY and expressly forbids any pre-authorisation process on that information - consistent with eIDAS recital wording that registration 'should not entail a pre-authorisation process'; (c) Art.8(1) is replaced so that Member States SHALL authorise at least one certificate authority to issue wallet-relying-party REGISTRATION certificates, and shall ensure those certificates are issued in an automated manner and without undue delay after registration. Annexes I, IV and V of CIR 2025/848 are amended. | [EU Publications Office (CELLAR), official OJ text of Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1730, CELEX 32026R1730](http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg_impl/2026/1730/oj) | 15/07/2026 | This changes the shape of the bank-side RP registration sell in all ten EU footprint markets. Registration is now an AUTOMATED, no-pre-authorisation filing - so the consulting value is NOT in shepherding an approval, it is in (a) getting the entitlement and requested-attribute declaration right, because it is published for transparency and is what supervisors and wallet users will see, and (b) building the RP endpoint that enforces attribute minimisation against that declaration. And because registration certificates are now MANDATORY for at least one CA per Member State (upgraded from 'Member States may require' in the original CIR 2025/848), every footprint bank will receive a registration certificate automatically and must be able to present and have it validated. Printec route: Namirial onboarding/eKYC plus RP middleware, Thales HSM/PCI for RP access-certificate key custody, plus integration and evidence packs. WHEN BUDGET COMMITS: H2-2026 to H1-2027. | High - in force since 11/08/2026, text on the face of the OJ | Medium | M | Medium | Read amended Annex I points 7-10 to specify exactly what entitlement/attribute data a bank must declare, and amended Annexes IV and V for the certificate profiles. Then, per footprint Member State, identify the authorised registration-certificate CA - that list is the integration map. |
| European Commission - Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1735 (amends the attestation-of-attributes act) | EU (AT,BG,HR,CY,CZ,GR,HU,RO,SK,SI) | NEW ROW ADDED BY THE VERIFIER - named in the ARF 3.0.0 release notes, absent from the researcher's set. CARRIES A HARD NEAR-TERM DATE THE SET DID NOT HAVE: 'Article 1(3) shall apply from 1 January 2027.' Article 1(3) amends Art.9 of CIR 2025/1569 - it inserts a new Art.9(2a) requiring the attribute VERIFICATION MECHANISM against an authentic source to comply with the specifications in the new Annex IV, and replaces Art.9(4) so that the verification result must name the public sector body responsible for the authentic source and must be SIGNED OR SEALED by that body (or its nationally recognised designated intermediary) using at least an advanced electronic signature based on a qualified certificate, or an advanced electronic seal based on a qualified certificate. The act also tightens Art.3(2) (issuers of qualified EAA and of public-sector EAA must comply with the corresponding registered scheme in the catalogue of schemes, as part of conformity assessment) and Art.4(3) (revocation duties for attestations valid more than 24 hours). Annexes I and II replaced, Annex IV added. Cryptographic evaluation may use Common Criteria v3.1 only until 31/08/2027, then CC:2022. WHAT: an attribute-verification mechanism conforming to the new Annex IV, plus signing/sealing of every verification result with advanced signatures/seals on qualified certificates - i.e. a qualified-certificate key-custody and signing service. Printec route: Thales HSM/PCI for the signing keys, Namirial for the QEAA/trust-service leg, integration services for the authentic-source connector. WHEN BUDGET COMMITS: NOW - a 01/01/2027 application date means the 6-12 month budget window is already open and closing. FROM 09/08/2026 dual display is voluntary and informational only (the euro price must be unambiguously the sole selling and payable price) BUT: (a) the ban on economically unjustified price increases continues from 09/08/2026 to 09/08/2027 under the CONSUMER PROTECTION ACT (Закон за защита на потребителите), enforced by KZP, with the burden on the trader to prove a direct and proportionate link to objective cost factors (supply/production costs, labour, energy, fuel, raw materials, tax and fee changes, exchange rates or other external economic conditions), and a property sanction of up to EUR 100,000, applied separately per good, service or outlet; (b) the PUBLIC PRICE PORTAL is being changed to publish WHOLESALE prices and an 'indicative fair value' alongside retail prices, with daily price-data publication MANDATORY for traders with turnover above EUR 25 MILLION in the preceding financial year and voluntary below that threshold. | [evroto.bg (Ministry of Finance official portal) — 'КЗП извърши над 14 500 проверки през периода на двойно обозначаване на цените' [KZP carried out over 14,500 inspections during the dual price display period], 10/08/2026; and 'НАП и КЗП извършиха рекорден брой проверки във връзка с еврото' [NRA and KZP carried out a record number of euro-related inspections], 13/08/2026, evroto.bg/bg/news/462](https://evroto.bg/bg/news/461-kzp-izvarshi-nad-14-500-proverki-prez-proverki-prez-perioda-na-dvoyno-oboznachav) | 13/08/2026 | Merchant-side rather than bank-side, but a live POS and fiscal-device requirement in a Printec market: fiscal-receipt content rules changed on 09/08/2026 (euro-only selling price; dual display now optional and visually subordinated), so every ECR/POS and fiscal-printer configuration in Bulgaria needs a release, and large retailers (>EUR 25m turnover) acquire a NEW daily price-data reporting obligation to the public price portal. Printec: POS terminals (Verifone/Castles) plus terminal management (TMS) mass-configuration and estate update; the >EUR 25m daily-reporting cohort is an integration opportunity. NOTE: the substantive price-control questions and the trader's burden of proof are a contested regulatory-interpretation matter — Printec's Compliance team must be consulted before any customer-facing claim about what the rules require. | Certain — in force since 09/08/2026 and running to 09/08/2027 | Medium | M | Medium | Obtain the amended Consumer Protection Act text and the secondary act setting the price-portal wholesale / indicative-fair-value fields, and confirm the daily-reporting file format for the >EUR 25m cohort. | ESMA + all EU National Competent Authorities | EU-wide (AT, BG, CY, CZ, GR, HR, HU, RO, SI, SK) | MiCA transitional period ENDED 1 JULY 2026 EU-wide. ESMA Public Statement ESMA75-113276571-1710 of 23/06/2026 calls on unauthorised CASPs to wind down in an orderly manner. Confirmed verbatim from the PDF: unauthorised CASPs must (a) immediately stop onboarding new EU clients, refrain from opening new client relationships or accounts, and cease marketing activities and solicitation; (b) limit services to actions necessary to sell or transfer crypto-assets, reallocate assets, or close positions, for the period strictly necessary to complete an orderly exit; (c) communicate clearly, promptly and repeatedly with retail and institutional clients, including a deadline by which residual positions would be closed automatically. Wind-down must comply with all relevant EU/national conduct law and AML/CFT obligations, and CASPs must maintain effective AML/CFT controls THROUGHOUT the wind-down - customer due diligence (CDD), transaction monitoring, screening against restrictive-measures and sanctions lists, suspicious transaction and activity reporting, and record-keeping. ESMA states this applies irrespective of whether national law has been adjusted to MiCA in a Member State. Builds on the earlier ESMA statement ESMA75-113276571-1679 (published under a 2026-04 path, cited in footnote 1). | [ESMA Public Statement ESMA75-113276571-1710 'ESMA calls on unauthorised crypto-asset service providers to wind down orderly ... as MiCA transitional period ends' (PDF, text extracted locally)](https://www.esma.europa.eu/sites/default/files/2026-06/ESMA75-113276571-1710_Public_Statement_MiCA_transitional_period_ends.pdf) | 23/06/2026 | Two concrete demand pulses: (a) authorised CASPs and bank crypto arms onboarding migrating client books at volume - Namirial digital onboarding/eKYC; (b) both winding-down and receiving firms must run AML/CFT for the whole exit - IMTF Siron / FICO AML-KYC and INETCO transaction monitoring. I downloaded the NBU PDF and extracted the full text. Confirmed: NBU Board Resolution No. 85 of 05 August 2026, marked 'Офіційно опубліковано 06.08.2026', amending the Instruction on the execution of interbank payment operations in Ukraine in the national currency approved by NBU Board Resolution No. 16 of 03 March 2023; signed by Governor Andrii Pyshnyi; the Department of Payment Systems and Innovative Development (Andrii Poddieriohin) is directed to notify Ukrainian PSPs; control assigned to Deputy Governor Oleksii Shaban. It adds a definition of the TrekSEP message and of TrekSEP itself (an auxiliary information service enabling tracking of SEP payment-operation stages), and a definition of 'учасник миттєвого переказу СЕП' [SEP instant transfer participant]. New point 46-4 obliges every SEP participant to send the payment-operation execution status to the SEP central processor (TsOSEP) as soon as possible and NO LATER THAN ONE HOUR after that status is set in its own automation system AND in the automation system of any non-bank PSP executing the operation through a settlement account held with that participant. New point 46-5 obliges the participant to (1) enable the user to query payment status by remote channel by sending a TrekSEP message to TsOSEP, and (2) return the execution-stage information received from TrekSEP to the user by remote channel. CORRECTION - THE BASELINE PUT ALL THREE OBLIGATIONS AT 01/01/2027. Point 4 of the resolution defers only 'абзаців п'ятого - сьомого підпункту 3 пункту 1' [paragraphs five to seven of sub-point 3 of point 1] to 01 January 2027; everything else took effect the day after official publication, i.e. 07/08/2026. Paragraphs five to seven are point 46-5 and its two sub-points ONLY. THE ONE-HOUR STATUS-REPORTING DUTY TO TsOSEP (POINT 46-4) IS PARAGRAPH FOUR AND HAS THEREFORE BEEN IN FORCE SINCE 07/08/2026 - it is already binding, not a 2027 deadline. | [НБУ - Постанова Правління НБУ № 85 від 05.08.2026 [NBU Board Resolution No. 85 of 05/08/2026] (PDF, full text extracted)](https://bank.gov.ua/admin_uploads/law/05082026_85.pdf) | 05/08/2026 | STRONGER THAN THE BASELINE STATED. | PSR Articles 59a / 59b - cross-sectoral fraud data sharing with telcos and online platforms | EU footprint (all 10 EU markets) | CONFIRMED VERBATIM at ST 8221/26 pp.295-300. Art.59a(-1): where payment fraud originates in the publication of fraudulent content online, PSPs 'shall, without undue delay, inform providers of hosting services following the procedure laid down in Article 16, or, where applicable, Article 22 of Regulation (EU) 2022/2065' (the Digital Services Act notice-and-action and trusted-flagger routes). Art.59a(1) permits data exchange between PSPs and hosting-service providers, and between PSPs and providers of electronic communications services (Directive (EU) 2018/1972), 'when there are objectively justified grounds to suspect fraudulent behaviour by a user of their service'. Art.59a(2) obliges electronic communications service providers and very large online platforms/search engines (DSA Art.33) to 'establish dedicated communication channels with payment service providers, or participate in a system for effective communication or in an information sharing mechanism'. Art.59b(2) obliges VLOPs/VLOSEs to request an authorisation number, registration number or equivalent from every advertiser of a 'regulated financial service', and Art.59b(2a) requires them to make best efforts - including by using the PSD3 registers - to assess whether that information is reliable, using automated tools where proportionate. TWO PROVISIONS THE RESEARCHER MISSED, both of which shift burden AWAY from banks: Art.59a(3) puts educational and alerting duties on telcos and VLOPs/VLOSEs (how to identify fraud attempts, precautions, reporting route), and Art.59a(5) obliges electronic communications providers to 'take appropriate organisational and technical measures to detect and prevent the use of their services for impersonation fraud, including by means of manipulation of calling line identification or electronic mail address'. Art.59a(4) has the Commission and the European Board for Digital Services facilitating a voluntary EU code of conduct. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8221/26 (PSR final compromise, LIMITE), Articles 59a and 59b](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: all PSPs in the 10 EU footprint markets. WHAT THEY MUST BUY OR CHANGE: an OUTBOUND fraud-intelligence interface bolted onto the fraud case-management stack - structured DSA Article 16 notice-and-action submissions to hosting providers, plus the telco and platform channels required by Art.59a(2). No incumbent CEE fraud platform ships this today. PRINTEC LANE: IMTF Siron and FICO case management extended with an outbound notification module; INETCO as the detection feed. BUDGET COMMIT: 9-12 months before EIF+21m. GDPR/ePRIVACY: this is personal-data sharing with non-financial third parties under GDPR and Directive 2002/58/EC. Printec's DPO / Compliance team must scope the lawful basis before any customer design work - this row must not be used to give a customer a compliance opinion. SIZING NOTE: Art.59a(3) and (5) put material detection and education duties on telcos and platforms rather than banks, so the bank-side spend is the notification interface, not end-to-end scam detection. | High - agreed text; the open variables are the calendar date, the final numbering, and whether an EU-level shared mechanism removes the build | Medium | M | — | Track whether an EU-level PSP fraud information-sharing mechanism emerges (EBA or EPC) that customers can join rather than build - Art.59a(2) expressly allows participation in such a mechanism as an alternative to bilateral channels, which would collapse this opportunity.
