| PSD3 Article 38 - registration regime for independent ATM deployers (IADs), with DORA evidence duty | EU footprint (all 10 EU markets) | CONFIRMED VERBATIM at ST 8222/26 pp.145-146, and the researcher's correction of the baseline is right: the registration article is in PSD3, not PSR. Art.38 is headed 'Services enabling cash withdrawals offered by ATM deployers not servicing payment accounts'. Art.38(1): 'ATM deployers shall not be subject to authorisation but shall register, before taking up activity, with the competent authority of the Member State where the cash withdrawal services are intended to be provided.' Art.38(2) requires the Art.3(3) application dossier at points (a), (b), (e)-(h), (j)-(l), (n), (p) and (q), including a description of audit arrangements and of organisational arrangements ensuring 'continuity and reliability in the performance of the payment service'; and the security control and mitigation measures under Art.3(3)(j) 'shall indicate how the person registering will ensure a high level of digital operational resilience in accordance with Chapter II of Regulation (EU) 2022/2554' (DORA - the Digital Operational Resilience Act), 'in particular in relation to technical security and data protection, including for the software and ICT systems used by the person registering OR THE UNDERTAKINGS TO WHICH IT OUTSOURCES the whole or part of its operations.' Art.38(2a) lets the competent authority refuse or revoke registration for non-compliance, with reasons. Art.38(3) disapplies Sections 1 and 2 of Chapter 1 (authorisation) but applies Section 3, except Arts.25(3) and 30. Art.38(4): registered deployers 'shall be treated as payment institutions'. VERIFIER FINDING ON THE RESEARCHER'S OPEN QUESTION: I checked PSD3 Arts.44 and 45 and found NO transitional provision for ATM deployers already operating at the transposition date - the transitional articles cover PSD2-authorised payment institutions, PSD2 Art.32 exemption holders and e-money institutions only. On the face of the compromise text an existing IAD must be registered before continuing activity at EIF+21m, with no run-off. That is a harder deadline than the researcher assumed. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8222/26 (PSD3 final compromise, LIMITE), Article 38, cross-checked against Articles 44 and 45](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8222-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: independent ATM deployers operating in the footprint's 10 EU markets - a buyer segment Printec does not currently serve as a regulated category. WHAT THEY MUST BUY OR CHANGE: register with each national competent authority before continuing activity, and evidence DORA Chapter II ICT risk management - including for outsourced operations, which makes the deployer's outsourced service partner part of the regulatory dossier. PRINTEC LANE, and this is the genuinely new angle: ATM/device telemetry and monitoring as the DORA Chapter II evidence layer, physical ATM security, managed and field services, x-core for standardised control and reporting. Printec can position as the outsourced partner whose controls the dossier can cite. BUDGET COMMIT: 9-12 months before EIF+21m, and earlier than for banks because registration must be in place BEFORE activity continues and no transitional run-off was found in the text. | High - the Art.38 text is agreed; open variables are the calendar date, national registration procedure, and whether a transitional for existing deployers is added at legal-linguistic revision or in national transposition | Medium | M | — | Build the footprint IAD target list from verifiable sources - the researcher asserted Euronet is 'the dominant IAD across Greece, Romania, Croatia, Hungary and the Czech Republic' with no citation, so establish this from national ATM-operator registers or Euronet's own filings before using it. Assemble a DORA-Chapter-II evidence pack for outsourced ATM estates. Re-check whether any IAD transitional appears in the OJ text or in national transposition drafts. |
| PSR Article 38 - open-banking dedicated-interface availability and performance | EU footprint (all 10 EU markets) | TEXT CONFIRMED VERBATIM at ST 8221/26 pp.248-250, BUT THE 'WHAT CHANGES' FRAMING IS CORRECTED. Confirmed: Art.38(1) - unavailability 'shall be presumed to have arisen when five consecutive requests for access to information for the provision of payment initiation services or account information services receive server error responses or no response from the account servicing payment service provider's dedicated interface within 30 seconds'. Art.38(2) - planned unavailability must be notified to PISPs/AISPs at least one month in advance, except emergency changes, and 'shall normally occur between 00:00 and 06:00'. Art.38(2a) - unplanned outages require timely notice of restoration measures and estimated resolution time, and ASPSPs 'shall ensure an optimal recovery time'. Art.38(2b) - the dedicated interface must provide 'availability and performance that are equal at least to those of the interface that the account servicing payment service provider uses for authentication and communication with its users'. Art.38(5) mandates EBA regulatory technical standards (RTS - binding Level-2 detail) on the quarterly availability/performance statistics under Art.35(5) and on the 'optimal recovery time' standard graded by incident severity, to be submitted to the Commission '[nine months after the date of entry into force of this Regulation]'. CORRECTION TO THE RESEARCHER'S FRAMING: they described this as moving from 'PSD2's soft 90-day KPI regime' to quarterly public statistics. Quarterly availability and performance statistics are ALREADY required under the PSD2 RTS (Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2018/389, Art.32). And parity is already imposed by PSR Art.37(1), which requires the dedicated interface to offer 'at all times at least the same level of availability and performance, including technical and IT support' as the customer interface. What is genuinely NEW in Art.38 is the objective five-requests/30-seconds outage presumption, the one-month notice and 00:00-06:00 maintenance window, and the severity-graded 'optimal recovery time' standard. DRAFTING DEFECT WORTH NOTING: Art.38(5)(b) cross-refers to 'paragraph 3', but the compromise text has no paragraph 3 in Art.38 - a renumbering artefact that will be fixed at legal-linguistic revision. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8221/26 (PSR final compromise, LIMITE), Articles 37 and 38](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: account-servicing PSPs in the 10 EU footprint markets that operate a dedicated open-banking interface. WHAT THEY MUST BUY OR CHANGE: the ability to MEASURE API-versus-customer-channel parity continuously and to detect the objective outage condition (five consecutive errors or no response within 30 seconds) as it happens, plus a maintenance-scheduling change to the 00:00-06:00 window with one month's notice. This is an observability and telemetry buy, not a compliance-document buy. PRINTEC LANE: INETCO transaction monitoring extended to API traffic; device and service telemetry and monitoring. TIMING ADVANTAGE: the nine-month EBA RTS deadline is the first hard Level-2 date after entry into force, so the specification lands roughly 12 months before the obligation applies - a rare case where the detail arrives before the deadline. BUDGET COMMIT: 9-12 months before EIF+21m, and the measurement capability should be bought early because a bank cannot scope remediation until it can see its own parity gap. | High - agreed text; the RTS content and the calendar date are the open variables | Medium | M | — | Watch for the EBA consultation on the Art.38(5) RTS (due nine months after entry into force). Ask footprint ASPSPs whether they can measure API-versus-app parity at all today - most publish PSD2 quarterly statistics without instrumenting real parity, and that gap is the sale. |

(c) WHEN TO COMMIT BUDGET: for pilot PSPs it is already committed; for the merchant-acceptance wave it follows the merchant call. | MNB — Recommendation 6/2025 (VI.16.) on the treatment of customers with disabilities | Hungary | [STANDING — dated 2025] MNB Recommendation 6/2025 (VI.16.) on the treatment of customers with disabilities, signed by Governor Varga Mihaly, has applied to supervised financial institutions since 28/06/2025 (point 35), the same day the EAA obligations bit, and it repealed Recommendation 4/2017 (III.13.) on that date (point 36). It is soft law — non-binding — but point 33 states that MNB 'figyelemmel kiseri es ertekeli' [monitors and assesses] compliance across supervised institutions in its inspection and monitoring activity, in line with general European supervisory practice. Its definitions expressly treat an ATM ('bankjegykiado automataja') as an 'ugyfelszolgalati pont' [customer service point] alongside branches and sales counters. It sits ON TOP of Hungary's EAA transposition, Act XVII of 2022 on the general rules of compliance with accessibility requirements for products and services ('Akadalymentessegi tv.'): MNB states it expects supervised firms within that Act's scope to follow the recommendation's supervisory expectations IN ADDITION to the statutory accessibility requirements, and it extends the expectations to cross-border services into Hungary. Concrete expectations that touch hardware and estate data: point 4 — record, in the Annex 1 template table, the conditions of equal-opportunity access for every customer service point the firm operates, broken down by special device, technology, service and infrastructure, and publish the full service map accessibly at each point, in the mobile app, on the website and via a dedicated call-centre menu option; point 5 — make available, broken down by county (varmegye), settlement and Budapest district, which customer service points are accessibly usable and accessibly reachable and by what means, adding that it is GOOD PRACTICE for branch- and ATM-finder interfaces to show which customer groups (deaf and hard of hearing, partially sighted and blind, mobility-impaired) each branch and ATM serves; the PIN-entry expectation — where a PIN must be entered on a touch keypad or touchscreen, use a device that also has SPEECH SUPPORT, or provide a push-button entry device, and failing that a tactile mask over the touchscreen keypad; point 25 — build websites and smart-device applications to the current EN 301 549 standard and WCAG guidelines. BASELINE CORRECTION STANDS: the baseline described Hungary's EAA framework via Gov. Decree 605/2022; the underlying transposition statute is Act XVII of 2022, as cited by MNB itself. | [MNB — 'A Magyar Nemzeti Bank 6/2025. (VI.16.) szamu ajanlasa a fogyatekossaggal elo ugyfelekkel kapcsolatos banasmodrol' (PDF re-downloaded, text re-extracted locally with pdfminer; signed Varga Mihaly, Governor)](https://www.mnb.hu/letoltes/6-2025-fogyatekossagal-elo-ugyfelek-ajanlas.pdf) | 16/06/2025 [STANDING — dated 2025] | A supervisory hook independent of the EAA statute. The speech-support-on-PIN-entry expectation is a direct spec for accessible POS PIN pads (Verifone / Castles with audio guidance) and for accessible ATM encrypting PIN pads, and it reaches acquirers' merchant estates as well as bank self-service. Points 4 and 5 force every Hungarian bank to inventory its estate machine by machine and publish per-point accessibility attributes down to settlement level — a natural entry point for an ATM telemetry / estate-audit engagement and for x-core to deliver one consistent accessible UI across mixed hardware. The 1,039 rural machines mandated under the ATM Act must satisfy this too, so the two Hungarian rows compound. | Applies now; enforced through supervisory dialogue and inspection findings rather than fines, so the driver is examination pressure and the estate inventory it forces. | Medium | M | — | Check MNB consumer-protection decisions (mnb.hu hatarozatok) for accessibility findings against named banks since 28/06/2025 — none surfaced in this run. |
| Ministerul Economiei (market surveillance for ATMs/payment terminals) + Autoritatea Nationala pentru Protectia Consumatorilor (ANPC, consumer banking services) | Romania | [STANDING — dated 2022] AUTHORITY SPLIT CLARIFIED — and it is not the one most people assume. Romania's EAA transposition is Legea nr. 232 din 19 iulie 2022 privind cerintele de accesibilitate aplicabile produselor si serviciilor, promulgated by Decree 1.007/2022 and published in Monitorul Oficial nr. 743 of 25/07/2022, in force 28/06/2025 (Art.30), with only Art.4(8) delayed to 28/06/2027. Verified in the law text: (i) Art.2(1)(e) covers 'terminale de plata pentru autoservire' and Art.2(2)(a) names 'bancomate' [ATMs], with ticket machines, check-in machines and interactive information terminals, for products placed on the market after 28/06/2025; consumer banking services are in scope under Art.2(3)(c); (ii) Art.19(1)(b) designates the MINISTRY OF THE ECONOMY — not ANPC and not the BNR — as market-surveillance authority for the products in Art.2(1)(e) and Art.2(2), i.e. self-service payment terminals and ATMs, while Art.19(1)(a) gives ANPC the other product categories AND consumer banking services under Art.2(3)(c); ANCOM, the Authority for the Digitalisation of Romania and the National Audiovisual Council take the remaining service categories. THE BANCA NATIONALA A ROMANIEI HAS NO ROLE under this law; (iii) fines are LOW — Art.27(1) sets RON 6,000–12,000 and RON 7,000–15,000 bands for manufacturer/importer/distributor breaches (with a further RON 5,000–10,000 band), and Art.27(3) sets RON 2,500–5,000 for improper application of the conformity marking PLUS withdrawal from the market and/or prohibition on making the product available; Art.27(4) requires the Art.19(1) authorities to notify the European Commission without delay of penalties imposed and complementary measures proposed; (iv) Art.29(1) gives the 28/06/2030 service transition and the five-year cap on pre-28/06/2025 contracts, and Art.29(2) allows self-service terminals used before 28/06/2025 to continue for no more than 20 years after entry into use; (v) Annex example (ix) is explicit: 'includerea unei mufe si a unui software intr-un bancomat, ceea ce va permite conectarea unei casti audio care va primi textul de pe ecran sub forma de sunet'. | [Legea nr. 232/2022 privind cerintele de accesibilitate aplicabile produselor si serviciilor, Monitorul Oficial nr. 743 of 25/07/2022, promulgated by Decree 1.007/2022 — full text read via a Wolters Kluwer Sintact.ro reproduction mirrored as PDF (legislatie.just.ro, cdep.ro and the EUR-Lex national-transposition page were all unreachable or JS-blocked from this environment; see verifier_note)](https://univ-danubius.ro/files/data/Legea%20232_2022%20privind%20cerintele%20de%20accesibilitate%20aplicabile%20produselor%20si%20serviciilor.pdf) | 25/07/2022 [STANDING — dated 2022] | Practical consequence for Printec Romania: an ATM or payment-terminal accessibility complaint runs through the MINISTRY OF THE ECONOMY on product-safety logic — CE marking, EU declaration of conformity, technical file, market withdrawal — while the BANK's service-level accessibility sits with ANPC. So Printec must supply BCR, BRD-Societe Generale, Banca Transilvania, ING Romania, Raiffeisen Bank Romania, UniCredit Bank Romania, CEC Bank and Alpha Bank Romania with a complete conformity dossier per ATM and terminal model, because the sanction that bites is not the RON 15,000 fine but the Art.27(3) power to PROHIBIT or WITHDRAW a model from the Romanian market, which would strand an in-flight rollout. BCR is already an APTRA reference account. The 20-year backstop plus the 28/06/2030 service transition means the refresh case must be built into 2027–2028 budgets. Article 4: enters into force the third day after publication = 27/07/2026, no deferred application date. It rewrites AI Act Art 113 third paragraph so Chapter III Sections 1-3 (high-risk provider/deployer obligations) apply from (i) 02/12/2027 for Annex III high-risk systems and (ii) 02/08/2028 for Annex I product-embedded high-risk systems — a delay from the original 02/08/2026. The 02/12/2026 date carried in the baseline is NOT the high-risk phase-in: it is when new Art 5(1) points (ba) and (bb) and Art 5(1a)/(1b) prohibitions start to apply, and the date by which providers of AI systems generating synthetic audio/image/video/text placed on the market before 02/08/2026 must comply with the Art 50(2) marking obligation. Arts 102-110 apply from 27/07/2026. Legacy public-authority high-risk deployers get until 02/08/2030 (amended Art 111(2)). | [Official Journal of the EU — Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (Digital Omnibus on AI); full text and metadata retrieved via Publications Office cellar, EUR-Lex web UI bot-blocked](http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2026/1744/oj) | 24/07/2026 | (a) AFFECTED: Printec and its bank customers deploying AI-touching lines — AML/KYC (IMTF Siron, FICO), transaction monitoring (INETCO), digital onboarding/eKYC with biometric matching (Namirial) — against Annex III high-risk categories (creditworthiness assessment, remote biometric identification). (b) BUY/CHANGE: the real compliance wall is 02/12/2027, not 02/12/2026, which buys roughly a year. But 02/12/2026 IS a genuine near-term date for any deployment generating synthetic content (chatbot or summarisation layers in onboarding or servicing) under Art 50(2) marking. (c) BUDGET COMMITS: H1-2027 for high-risk conformity work; NOW for Art 50(2) content-marking, which is 15 months closer. CORRECTION TO THE RESEARCHER'S NUMBERING: requirement 8, not 9, is structured agent identifiers such as BICs; requirement 9 is entity identification via name and postal address, BICs or LEIs; requirement 10 is person identification; requirement 11 is 'To provide a common minimum level of postal address information structured to the extent possible', implemented as 'Usage of town name and country data elements as a minimum, complemented by further address information'. | Central Bank of Montenegro (CBCG) — unilateral euroisation | Montenegro | [STANDING — 2002 regime; EUREP line since 31/07/2020] Refreshed primary source, relevant because it defines what 'euro adoption' does and does NOT mean in the Western Balkans. CBCG's own euroisation page: the German Mark became the sole legal tender in January 2001 and 'since March 2002 the official means of payment has become the Euro'; use of the euro is handled inside EU accession Chapter 17 'Economic and monetary policy', opened at the Intergovernmental Conference on 25/06/2018; and CBCG states that 'the use of the euro as the legal tender in Montenegro differs entirely from the membership in the euro area', euroisation being a specific case because Montenegro entered the regime BEFORE the ECOFIN Council adopted its position prohibiting unilateral euroisation. Operationally CBCG cannot use monetary policy to affect money supply, must incorporate ECB policy and 'imports' euro-area monetary policy; because it is not an ESCB member it has no direct access to the emergency liquidity assistance (ELA) arrangements available to ESCB national central banks. Its substitutes: it 'regularly renews the use of the Eurosystem repo facility for central banks (EUREP) line as a precautionary backstop', an EU instrument established in June 2020 and available to Montenegro as of 31/07/2020, permitting drawdown of UP TO EUR 250 MILLION for systemic liquidity at short notice, plus a renewable BIS repo credit line of EUR 100 MILLION; domestic credit support to solvent institutions is constrained to highly credible collateral, limiting both collateral type and the size of the support. | [Centralna banka Crne Gore / Central Bank of Montenegro — 'Euroisation' (Core functions > Monetary Policy)](https://www.cbcg.me/en/core-functions/monetary-policy/euroisation) | 31/07/2020 | NO changeover opportunity in Montenegro — the cash has been euro for 24 years, so there is no dual-display, frontloading, redenomination or validator-reprofiling work of the Bulgarian kind anywhere in the euroised Western Balkans (Montenegro, Kosovo). In reverse, Montenegrin and Kosovan ATMs already dispense and recycle euro, which makes them candidates for ECB-list tested recyclers and fitness sorting on a purely commercial rather than regulatory-forced basis. The real Printec lever in Montenegro is elsewhere: TIPS Clone instant-payments onboarding, and the liquidity-constrained environment CBCG itself describes (no ELA access, capped EUREP/BIS backstops) which puts a premium on efficient cash working capital — APTRA OptiCash and recycling ATMs to reduce idle euro cash in the estate, a directly quantifiable argument in a country that cannot print its own money. | Standing regime; no change expected before EU accession | Medium | S | Low | Check the CBCG Instant Payments (TIPS Clone) page for the Montenegrin bank onboarding list and dates, and the Commission's 2026 Enlargement Package Chapter 17 assessment for any change in the EU position on Montenegrin euroisation at accession. |

| PSR Article 88a - FRAND interoperability access to mobile-device payment features | EU footprint (all 10 EU markets) | CONFIRMED VERBATIM at ST 8221/26 pp.365-366. Art.88a(1): 'Without prejudice to Article 6(7) of Regulation (EU) 2022/1925' (the Digital Markets Act), original equipment manufacturers of mobile devices and electronic communications service providers within the meaning of Article 2(4) of Directive (EU) 2018/1972 'shall allow payment service providers and technical services providers acting on their behalf effective interoperability with, and access for the purposes of interoperability to, the technical features, such as hardware features and software features, that are necessary to processing and executing securely payment transactions, on fair, reasonable and non-discriminatory terms.' Art.88a(2) preserves OEMs' ability to take 'strictly necessary and proportionate measures' to protect the integrity of those features, provided they are duly justified. Art.88a(3) requires OEMs and telcos to 'publish general conditions of effective interoperability and access'. FRAND = fair, reasonable and non-discriminatory. Note that unlike DMA Article 6(7), which binds only designated gatekeepers, Art.88a binds ALL mobile-device OEMs and electronic communications service providers. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8221/26 (PSR final compromise, LIMITE), Article 88a](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: footprint banks that wanted bank-branded mobile wallet or tap-to-pay functionality and were refused OEM access because the OEM was not a designated DMA gatekeeper. WHAT CHANGES: the access right extends beyond gatekeepers, and the text expressly names 'technical services providers acting on their behalf' - the category Printec occupies when it runs wallet, tokenisation or terminal integrations for a bank, which means Printec can invoke the access right in its own delivery role rather than only through the customer. PRINTEC LANE: POS terminals and acquiring (Verifone/Castles), terminal management, digital onboarding, wallet/tokenisation integration. BUDGET COMMIT: not a compliance spend for banks - it is an enabler that unblocks discretionary wallet projects, so it follows commercial appetite rather than a deadline; earliest realistic pull is around EIF+21m when the article applies. SIZING: kept small - the enforcement route and remedies are untested and no OEM FRAND terms exist yet. | Medium - the text is agreed, but the enforcement route and remedies are untested and depend on OEMs actually publishing workable FRAND terms | Medium | S | — | Watch for Commission or national enforcement guidance on Art.88a and for any OEM-published general conditions of interoperability and access under Art.88a(3) - until those exist this is an option, not a pipeline item. |

| PSR final compromise text, Article 112 (Entry into force and application) | EU (AT,BG,HR,CY,CZ,GR,HU,RO,SK,SI) | RE-READ IN THE PRIMARY PDF AND CONFIRMED VERBATIM. Council doc ST 8221/26 (Interinstitutional File 2023/0210(COD), Brussels, 17 April 2026, 'Confirmation of the final compromise text with a view to agreement'), Article 112 at p.428 sets a THREE-TIER clock: the Regulation 'shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union'; 'It shall apply from [OP please insert the date= 21 months after the date of entry into force of this Regulation]'; 'However, Articles 50 and 57 shall apply from [OP please insert the date= 27 months after the date of entry into force of this Regulation]'; 'Articles 85a and 108a shall apply from the date of entry into force of this Regulation.' Article 50 = payee name / unique-identifier matching (Verification of Payee, VoP - checking that the payee name the payer typed matches the account identifier). Article 57 = the PSP liability regime for getting that matching wrong. Recital 143 CONFIRMED VERBATIM as the rationale: the extra six months is granted 'thus granting payment service providers enough time to take the necessary steps to adjust their internal systems'. Article 85a re-read and confirmed as an SCA derogation for recurring credit transfers set up under a payer-payee agreement; Article 108a re-read and confirmed as a transitional carve-out for e-money-token / crypto payment transactions - both biting on entry into force. TWO CAVEATS THE RESEARCHER OMITTED: (1) ST 8221/26 is marked LIMITE - it is a Council working document, publicly retrievable but not the final legal text; (2) all dates are '[OP please insert]' placeholders and the 'a'-suffixed article numbers (50/57 are stable, but 59a, 85a, 88a, 108a are not) WILL be renumbered at legal-linguistic revision. Do not quote article numbers to customers as final. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8221/26 - final compromise text of the Payment Services Regulation (LIMITE)](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: all PSPs in the 10 EU footprint markets. WHAT CHANGES: two separate budget waves, not one. Wave 1 (EIF+21m) = ATM fee display (Art.7), SCA accessibility (Art.88), impersonation-fraud prevention and refund (Art.59), open-banking interface availability/performance (Art.38). Wave 2 (EIF+27m) = VoP-for-all-credit-transfers plus the Art.57 liability engine. Art.85a bites on day one of entry into force, so the SCA rules engine must be touched before any national transposition. PRINTEC ACTION: sell Wave 1 and Wave 2 as separate programmes with separate budget years - conflating them is how the 27-month tier gets under-funded. BUDGET COMMIT: Wave 1 at EIF+9 to EIF+12 months; Wave 2 at EIF+15 to EIF+18 months. | PSD3 final compromise text, Articles 44-45 and 48-50 (Transitional / Repeal / Transposition / Entry into force) | EU (AT,BG,HR,CY,CZ,GR,HU,RO,SK,SI) | CORRECTED. Re-read against the primary text (Council doc ST 8222/26, 2023/0209(COD), 17/04/2026). CONFIRMED: Art.49(1) requires Member States to adopt and publish the transposing laws 'by ... [21 months after entry into force of this Directive] at the latest'; Art.49(2) requires them to 'apply those measures from ... [21 months after entry into force of this Directive]' - publication and application coincide, there is no additional grace period. Art.50: the Directive 'shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal'. Art.48: PSD2 (Directive (EU) 2015/2366) and the E-Money Directive (2009/110/EC) are both repealed 'with effect from ... [21 months after entry into force of this Directive]'. Also confirmed: PSD3 Art.13(1) requires the EBA to adopt internal-governance guidelines '[one year after the date of entry into force of this Directive]'. CONTRADICTED AND CORRECTED - the researcher wrote that Articles 44-45 set the re-authorisation/grandfathering cut-off 'at the same 21-month date'. They do not. Art.44(1) lets payment institutions authorised under PSD2 Art.11 by EIF+21m continue WITHOUT a new authorisation UNTIL EIF+27 MONTHS, and requires competent authorities to complete their compliance assessment 'by ... [27] months after the date of entry into force'; non-compliant institutions are then suspended. Art.45 gives electronic money institutions the identical EIF+27m window. Art.44(3) gives PSD2 Art.32 small-payment-institution exemption holders the same EIF+27m run-off. So 21 months is the eligibility cut-off for WHO counts as an existing institution; 27 months is the actual re-authorisation deadline. That is a six-month difference in when the re-authorisation dossier work has to be finished. | [Council of the EU, doc ST 8222/26 - final compromise text of PSD3 (LIMITE)](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8222-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | AFFECTED: footprint national regulators (BNR-RO, BNB-BG, MNB-HU, CNB-CZ, NBS-SK, HNB-HR, Banka Slovenije, CBC-CY, BoG-GR, FMA-AT) plus every payment institution and e-money institution licensed in those markets. WHAT CHANGES: each regulator must run a transposition consultation, historically 9-15 months after OJ publication; that consultation window, not the EU date, is where national gold-plating (ATM density, accessibility add-ons, local fee-disclosure formats) appears and is Printec's earliest reliable demand signal. Headline: LIMITED PROGRESS on economic convergence since 2024, with external shocks (Russia's war against Ukraine, global trade tensions, the outbreak of war in the Middle East) named as the drag. By criterion — price stability: reference value 2.7% (from Cyprus 0.9%, France 1.2%, Denmark 1.6%, plus 1.5pp); Romania considerably above it, Hungary and Poland above it to a lesser extent, Czechia and Sweden below. Fiscal: Hungary, Poland and Romania exceeded the 3%-of-GDP deficit reference value in 2025; the number of footprint/reviewed countries under an excessive deficit procedure has risen to three, and NONE is projected to correct before end-2027 — Romania's EDP was opened in 2020 with the correction deadline EXTENDED TO 2030, Hungary's and Poland's were opened in July 2024 with deadlines of 2026 and 2028. Debt was below the 60% reference in all reviewed countries except Hungary in 2025, but the Commission projects Poland and Romania above 60% in 2026. Long-term interest rates: reference 5.1% (from Denmark 2.6%, Cyprus 3.1%, France 3.5%, average 3.1% plus 2pp); Poland 5.4%, Hungary 6.7%, Romania 6.7% all above. Exchange rate: 'none of the currencies of the countries under review is participating in the exchange rate mechanism (ERM II)'. Legal convergence: 'none of the five countries under review was fully compatible with the requirements for the adoption of the euro'. | ESRB (European Systemic Risk Board) / European Commission | EU-wide | [STANDING - Recommendation dated 25/09/2025; the live trigger is the 30/09/2026 consultation close] The substantive fight inside the MiCA review is THIRD-COUNTRY MULTI-ISSUANCE. Recommendation of the European Systemic Risk Board of 25 September 2025 on third-country multi-issuer stablecoin schemes (ESRB/2025/9), OJ ref C/2025/6342. Confirmed definition from the text: a 'third-country multi-issuer stablecoin scheme' means a stablecoin issuance model where a Union-based issuer collaborates with a non-Union issuer to jointly issue e-money tokens (EMTs) or asset-referenced tokens (ARTs) which share the same technical characteristics and are deemed interchangeable, where the issuers operate under different jurisdictions with the reserves divided between them. The ESRB's actual finding, quoted: 'While MiCAR regulates the intra-Union multi-issuance of interchangeable crypto-assets it does not explicitly address the possibility of schemes involving a Union issuer and a third-country issuer outside the Union.' The ESRB adds that because such schemes are not explicitly prohibited, some national competent authorities consider them allowed and others do not - a divergence running contrary to MiCAR's objective of uniform requirements and creating high legal uncertainty, given passporting. Recommendation G asks ESMA to foster supervisory convergence for CASPs given the risk of amplifying cross-border redemptions of fungible stablecoins; Recommendation H asks that Union issuers evidence that supporting financial institutions in the EU and in third countries can promptly execute cross-border asset sales. | [ESRB - Recommendation of the European Systemic Risk Board of 25 September 2025 on third-country multi-issuer stablecoin schemes (ESRB/2025/9), OJ C/2025/6342](https://www.esrb.europa.eu/pub/pdf/recommendations/esrb.recommendation251020.en.pdf) | 25/09/2025 | Indirect but real for Printec: if MiCA 2.0 tightens reserve-segregation, redemption and cross-border-readiness rules, EMT issuers - including a bank consortium such as Qivalis with RBI inside it - will need auditable reserve attestation, redemption-flow monitoring and segregation controls: INETCO transaction monitoring plus Thales HSM for reserve and redemption key control. Recommendation H's 'evidence that supporting financial institutions can promptly execute cross-border asset sales' is the specific hook, because it pushes the evidencing burden onto the banks holding the reserves. | High that multi-issuance is addressed in the Commission's post-consultation report; legislative change is 2027+ | Medium | Unscoped | — | Cross-reference ESRB/2025/9 Recommendations A-H against the consultation questionnaire; verify the EMT reserve-deposit percentage directly against MiCA Title IV rather than secondary commentary. |

### What changed in the calendar since 02/08/2026

1. **09/01/2027 restored (CZ/HU/RO instant receive).** The previous run moved it to 09/04/2027; that was wrong — the April date covers only e-money and payment institutions, not banks. Anything built off the 02/08 file needs correcting.
2. **Bulgaria's 01/01/2027 VoP date is no longer contested** — it is confirmed on the BNB's own SEPA FAQ. The OJ Article-5 citation is still unopened, so cite the BNB, not the Regulation.
3. **Hungary's rural-ATM mandate is fully primary-sourced now**: 31/12/2026 phase date in NGM Decree 16/2025 (V.29.), 1,039 named settlements in MNB Decree 19/2025 Annex 1. The press-sourced caveat is discharged.
4. **31/12/2026 DORA Register of Information is a reference date, not a filing deadline** — the real bank deadline is each NCA's own earlier cut-off, with NCAs reporting to the ESAs by 31/03/2027.
5. **Two baseline dates were REMOVED as unsupported:** Albania's 01/07/2027 DORA-mirror application date (the Bank of Albania press release gives no regulation number and no application date) and Bulgaria's "28/06/2027 EAA supervisory milestone" (that provision delays the 112 emergency-call rules, not anything touching ATMs or payment terminals). Ukraine's 13/12/2026 NBU transition end could not be confirmed either — Resolution No.143 exists and took effect 13/12/2025, but the 2026 date is not on the NBU legislation page.
6. **New dates that were not on the previous calendar:** the Cyber Resilience Act (11/09/2026 reporting, 11/12/2027 full) — which lands on Printec's own products, not only on banks; Austria's NISG 2026 date now verified by computation; the EUMSS managed-security certification consultation (13/09/2026); the EBA operational-risk RTS (31/12/2026); and the corrected AI Act phase-in dates.
7. **Slovenia's ATM density floor has not gone away, but it has no date.** The ZDUPS consultation closed 17/08/2026 with 23 responses, several proposing different cash-point criteria, and on 24/08/2026 the Ministry of Finance opened negotiations with Banka Slovenije and the Bank Association — no decisions, no revised criteria, no timetable. Keep engaging: the criteria are live and being renegotiated right now.
8. **The Western Balkans TIPS Clone November-2026 window still has no firm day and no named banks** — press-only, re-checked and unchanged. BiH now has five confirmed IPS participants (Intesa Sanpaolo Banka, Raiffeisen, Sparkasse, Nova Banka, ATOS Bank), up from three.

### Carried forward from 02/08/2026 but NOT re-verified this run

These sat outside the rows returned this cycle and must not be treated as re-confirmed: Ukraine's new UAH 2,000 banknote (04/09/2026 — a hard ATM/recycler validator date if correct); North Macedonia's single register of accounts (30/09/2026); the ECB banknote-design survey close (21/09/2026); and Serbia's Law on Information Security / NBS ICT regulations (31/10/2026 — nbs.rs serves only a JavaScript shell to every route tried). Next run should re-open each at its primary source.

For any question touching data-subject rights, a personal-data breach, or a contested reading of a
regulation, refer to Printec's DPO / Compliance team rather than relying on this file.


## Verification notes
- **Corrected/contradicted by the adversarial pass (6):** PSPs in Romania, Czechia and Hungary (non-euro EU Member States in the footprint) — CONTRADICTED. The OJ text of Art.5a(8) second subparagraph reads 'by 9 January 2027' for RECEIVING and 'by 9 July 2027' for SENDING by non-euro PSPs — the researcher asserted 9 April 2027 for receiving and used that to overturn a correct baseline entry. The 9 April 2027 date appears only in the FOURTH subparagraph, which is limited to electronic money institutions and payment institutions. Corrected value and source: Regulation (EU) No 260/2012 Art.5a(8) as inserted by Regulation (EU) 2024/886 Art.1(2), OJ L, 2024/886, 19.3.2024. Art.5b(3) (09/01/2027 for non-euro) and Art.5c(9) (09/07/2027 for non-euro) were stated correctly and are confirmed. The signal text and the deadline field have been rewritten: deadline moved from 09/07/2027 to 09/01/2027, which is the nearest binding date and materially changes the urgency of the row. Also added the Art.5a(8) third-subparagraph derogation running to 09/06/2028, which the researcher missed and which is the lever a non-euro bank will use to defer the sending build. Recital 10 (outside-business-hours permission) verified as described.; MONEYVAL / Serbia — APML and NBS-supervised banks (incl. Raiffeisen Serbia, AikBank) — Contradicted on the two load-bearing claims. (1) PUBLICATION DATE: the researcher reported adoption at the 70th Plenary in December 2025 with 'publication announced for June 2026' and gave source_date 12/06/2026. Serbia's own FIU published a detailed statement on the completed evaluation on 04/02/2026 including the direct link to the published MER, so the report was public roughly four months earlier than reported. (2) FRAMING: the researcher inferred 'enhanced follow-up' and 'sustained, dated pressure on Serbian banks' effectiveness'. The only primary-adjacent source obtainable — the national FIU — reports 7 positive ratings out of 11 immediate outcomes and a top-13 global placement. Enhanced-follow-up status is not stated anywhere I could reach and the negative framing is unsupported. Source swapped from the Cloudflare-blocked coe.int page to the APML statement, which I read in full. coe.int, rm.coe.int and the MER PDF itself all return 403 to curl with a desktop UA and to the fetch tool, so the ratings table could not be read and the follow-up question stays open. Opp_size cut from L to M and win from Medium to Low to match the corrected evidence.; Qivalis (Amsterdam JV) - incl. Raiffeisen Bank International (AT) — CONTRADICTED on multiple points after re-reading the CaixaBank issuer source. (1) source_date was recorded as '2026'; the item is dated 02/12/2025 - this is a nine-month-old announcement, not new, so is_new set to false. (2) Founding shareholders per the issuer are NINE - Banca Sella, CaixaBank, Danske Bank, DekaBank, ING, KBC, RBI, SEB, UniCredit. BBVA and DZ Bank are NOT named as founders and BNP Paribas is named as JOINING on 01/12/2025, not founding; the researcher listed all three as founding. (3) '37 banks across 15 countries' is unsupported - the source says ten banks. (4) 'has applied to DNB' overstates 'working towards DNB authorization'. (5) Fireblocks is NOT mentioned anywhere in the issuer source. Corrected on the upside: RBI's founding status and the H2-2026 launch window ARE issuer-confirmed, so those are no longer press-tier. Confidence cut to Low and opp_size cut L->M given how much of the original row did not survive.; Ministry of Finance of Slovenia + Banka Slovenije + Bank Association of Slovenia — all Slovenian banks and ATM deployers — CONTRADICTED on the central new claim, though the row survives. The researcher asserted the instrument was 're-scoped and RENAMED' on 17/07/2026 to 'Zakon o dostopnosti gotovine in brezplacnih osnovnih placilnih storitev'. CORRECTED VALUE: no such rename occurred. The 24/08/2026 gov.si note the researcher itself cites says the meeting concerned 'priprave predloga Zakona o dostopnosti in uporabi placilnih sredstev' — the ORIGINAL title from the 16/03/2026 draft — and the sibling item at https://www.gov.si/novice/2026-08-17-zakljucek-javne-obravnave-predloga-zakona-o-dostopnosti-in-uporabi-placilnih-sredstev/ (17/08/2026) uses the same title. Verified by raw-HTML curl of the gov.si page and by listing the Ministry of Finance news index, so this is not a summariser artefact. CONFIRMED elements: consultation closed 17/08/2026 (with 23 responses — a detail the researcher missed); the free-basic-payment-services limb; the 24/08/2026 dialogue with the Banka Slovenije working group and the Banking Association; and the verbatim Slovenian aims quotation, which matches character-for-character. The researcher's caution that the 60/100,000 and 5 km figures are draft-only and must not be quoted as law is CORRECT and independently reconfirmed — neither published item contains any numeric density requirement (the 17/08 item refers only to a 'mrezo gotovinskih tock' [network of cash points]). Minor: the quoted phrase 'nadaljevati konstruktiven dialog' is a paraphrase; the source reads 'Z bankami bomo nadaljevali konstruktiven dialog'. Opp size lowered L->M given no tabled bill and no verified density metric.; Komisia za finansov nadzor (FSC/KFN) + Balgarska narodna banka (BNB) — CONTRADICTED on the headline figure. The researcher reported 'only TWO Bulgarian firms are MiCA-licensed - Alaric Securities OOD and Belayer OOD'. The FSC's own register shows FOUR: Alaric Securities OOD (22/12/2025), Belayer LTD (28/04/2026), Altcoins BG EOOD (21/07/2026), Digital Assist OOD (21/07/2026). Signal text and source upgraded from press (Investor.bg) to the FSC register. Alaric being first is CONFIRMED. The 4-of-8 refusal rate, the 15%-of-turnover fine and the FSC/BNB competence split remain unverified press claims and are now flagged as such in the signal.; European Commission (DG FISMA) - IPR enforcement across the EU footprint — The France INFR(2026)2077 IPR-penalties procedure and the Spain INFR(2025)2140 SFD/PSD2 reasoned opinion are confirmed from the aggregator, along with the 04/06/2026 package date. CONTRADICTED: the same package's financial-services decisions include ROMANIA under INFR(2025)2040 (with Poland), so the baseline's headline claim - 'NO Printec-footprint Member State appears in the IPR/payments sections' - is false as stated and was corrected in the signal text. Aggregator-only: ec.europa.eu/commission/presscorner returned an 18KB JavaScript shell with no decision text to every route tried, so the Commission's own wording has never been read; confidence stays Low per the aggregator rule. The 10%-turnover / EUR 5m penalty figures and the 'Romania cured its exposure' inference are both unverified and were flagged rather than repeated as fact.
- **Unconfirmed (single/weak source, kept at Low) (9):** Republic of Bulgaria - Ministry of e-Government (draft Law on the European Digital Identity Wallet), Montenegro — Parliament / CBCG-supervised banks (CKB-OTP, NLB Banka, Erste Bank Montenegro, Hipotekarna banka, Lovcen banka), Hellenic Capital Market Commission (Epitropi Kefalaiagoras), Cyprus Securities and Exchange Commission (CySEC), Autoritatea de Supraveghere Financiara (ASF) / Government of Romania, Bulgaria — Bulgarian banks (UniCredit Bulbank, DSK/OTP, UBB/KBC, Postbank, Fibank), Governments of North Macedonia and Montenegro, Central Bank of Montenegro (CBCG), European Payments Council (EPC) - VoP and SEPA scheme rulebooks

**Operator requests — gated / paywalled / blocked sources to fetch or verify manually:**
- **DORA (digital operational resilience):** 1. EUR-LEX (bot-blocked to all fetch tools): open in a real browser and save the OJ PDF of Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2025/532 of 24/03/2025 (CELEX 32025R0532, DORA subcontracting RTS, OJ 02/07/2025) so the entry-into-force Article can be quoted verbatim. While there, also save Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2025/1190 of 13/02/2025 (TLPT RTS, DORA Art.26(11)) and Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2024/2956 (Register of Information templates). 2. BANK OF ALBANIA: obtain the numbered Regulation on "Digital Operational Resilience" adopted by the Supervisory Council on 01/07/2026 and the paired amendments to the Regulation on "Use of Information and Communication Technology by entities licensed by the Bank of Albania" — neither is yet on the public supervision-regulations index. Source from Fletorja Zyrtare (Albanian official gazette) or by written request to BoA. Needed to fix the regulation number and to VERIFY OR KILL the 01/07/2027 application date carried from the baseline. 3. CENTRAL BANK OF MONTENEGRO: cbcg.me press-centre listing 404s. Retrieve the CBCG Council decision and the Nacrt zakona o izmjenama i dopunama Zakona o kreditnim institucijama (Draft Law amending the Law on Credit Institutions) containing the DORA chapter, plus any Sluzbeni list CG publication, to replace the press-tier row with a primary one and establish an application date. 4. SERBIA: pull the full text of Odluka 43/2026 (Sluzbeni glasnik RS br. 43/2026) on remote establishment and verification of the identity of a natural person by electronic means, to establish its application date and whether fully automated (non-video) remote identification is permitted — this determines the admissible Namirial eKYC configuration for Serbia. 5. UKRAINE: obtain NBU Board Resolution No. 70 of 30/06/2026 from zakon.rada.gov.ua to extract the transition deadline for re-papering EXISTING supplier contracts under the new third-party-risk regime (the NBU news item says banks are given "sufficient time" but states no date). 6. NORTH MACEDONIA and BOSNIA & HERZEGOVINA: no ICT/operational-resilience instrument found this run. Sweep nbrm.mk regulatory decisions and the two BiH entity banking agencies (FBA Sarajevo, ABRS Banja Luka) for a 2025-2026 ICT-risk or outsourcing rulebook.
- **Instant payments & SEPA (incl. SCT Inst, VoP):** 1) EUR-Lex, Regulation (EU) 2024/886, Article 5 — a human should open https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/886/oj/eng and paste the verbatim text of the transitional article on Member States where the euro is introduced before 09/04/2027. This is the load-bearing citation for Bulgaria's 01/01/2027 date and the only thing standing between "Medium" and a promotable finding. 2) Monitorul Oficial text of OUG nr. 5/2026 (12/02/2026, Romania) — specifically the sanction levels, the competent authority, and whether BNR really holds an annually renewable one-year deferral power for instant euro obligations and on what test. 3) EPC Register of Participants for the SCT Inst and VoP schemes, filtered to Czech Republic, Hungary, Romania, Bulgaria and Cyprus — to replace press counts of live banks with a primary figure (europeanpaymentscouncil.eu 403s to automated fetching). Also download EPC VoP Scheme Rulebook v2.0 the day it publishes (target November 2026). 4) NBRNM (nbrm.mk) instant-payments announcements and the June-2026 NBRNM/World Bank workshop deck — the site is Cloudflare-gated to automated tools. 5) Bank of Albania press release corresponding to Governor Sejko's 05/08/2026 TIPS Clone remarks, plus the latest National Payments System Committee minutes and any commercial-bank onboarding calendar. 6) Montenegro (CBCG) instant-payments / TIPS Clone status — not covered this run because the search budget ran out; needs a fresh pass.
- **PSD3 / PSR:** 1) EUR-Lex access: an operator with a browser session should confirm from https://eur-lex.europa.eu whether any "Regulation (EU) 2026/... on payment services in the internal market" or "Directive (EU) 2026/... on payment services and electronic money services" has appeared in the OJ L series between 02/08/2026 and today. My independent evidence says no, but EUR-Lex is the only definitive check and it is bot-walled. 2) The European Parliament December 2026 plenary draft agenda (europarl.europa.eu/plenary) should be checked once published to confirm or move the 14/12/2026 indicative sitting date for procedures 2023/0209(COD) and 2023/0210(COD). 3) Nothing here is paywalled; no paid-subscription PDF is required.
- **ISO 20022 migration:** 1) Fetch the swift.com SR2026 pages from an unblocked network: /news-events/news/iso-20022-milestone-november-2026-unstructured-addresses-be-removed, /standards/iso-20022/removal-unstructured-address, /standards/iso-20022/iso-20022-bytes/call-action-november-2026, and the CBPR+ 'Removal of Unstructured Address - Community Requirements Info sheet' at swift.com/swift-resource/252559/download - needed to confirm the network-level NAK mechanic and the exempted message identifiers (admi.024, camt.025, camt.052, camt.053, camt.054, camt.060), plus the availability and commercial terms of the Swift AI address-structuring model. 2) Retrieve BNR ReGIS and TARGET-Romania content with a JS-capable browser (bnr.ro/2153-regis and /2155-target-romania) - Romania is the one footprint RTGS whose address position could not be established. 3) Locate the two May-2026 NBS Serbia Governor's acts on MT/MX coexistence in Sluzbeni glasnik RS - nbs.rs 404s on every payment-system path tried. 4) Obtain the Croatian SEPA Forum SEPA Newsletter br.18 (December 2025) PDF from sepa.hr. 5) Ask CBBH which EPC rulebook version is currently designated as current for Ziro kliring - that single answer decides whether 15/11/2026 binds BiH retail clearing. None of these are paywalled; all are access/rendering blocks.

Two useful-but-manual items an operator could fetch: (a) the Council of the EU digital euro policy timeline page (https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/policies/digital-euro/timeline/), which sits behind a JavaScript browser check and would confirm the trilogue calendar from a primary source rather than press; (b) the Sluzbeni glasnik RS No. 19/2025 PDF of the Law amending the Law on the National Bank of Serbia, to replace the aggregator citation for the digital dinar legal basis with the gazette text.
- **European Accessibility Act (ATMs/self-service):** (1) SLOVENIA — obtain the 23 consultation submissions on the ZDUPS draft, and specifically the Zdruzenje bank Slovenije (Bank Association) submission and any NLB / OTP banka Slovenije submission. These are not published on GOV.SI; an operator should request them from the Ministry of Finance under the access-to-public-information act (ZDIJZ) or via the e-demokracija portal. They will show exactly which density and geography numbers the banks are fighting and what adaptation period they asked for — the single most valuable input for sizing the Slovenian ATM opportunity. (2) CYPRUS — obtain the Council of Ministers decision under s.3(3) of N.57(I)/2024 designating the accessibility surveillance authorities. Likely in the Cyprus Government Gazette (Episimi Efimerida, Third Annex) or held by the Deputy Ministry of Social Welfare; it is not on CyLaw and may need a direct request. Without it, nobody knows who actually inspects a Cypriot ATM. (3) ECB — confirm whether an Opinion on the Slovenian draft Act on Accessibility and Use of Payment Instruments has been requested and/or issued, and locate the ECB's current opinions register URL (the old paths now 404). (4) HUNGARY — obtain from MNB the current status of the 1,039 mandated ATM sites (how many live, how many outstanding in the >500-inhabitant tranche due 31/12/2026) and any Act XVIII of 2025 s.2(5) fine decisions issued to date. This may need a formal MNB information request. (5) GREECE, SLOVAKIA, CZECHIA, AUSTRIA — the EAA transposition acts, designated market-surveillance authorities and penalty bands were not reached this run and should be commissioned as a follow-up unit, together with the non-EU cash-access/ATM-availability laws in RS, UA, AL, BA, MK, ME and XK.
